<meta>This page serves as the Preface to the translation of Phaladeepika. It establishes the author's credentials and the historical context of the work.</meta>

# PREFACE

<unclear>Astrology</unclear> is no longer looked upon with derision. Interest in the study of ancient sciences has so far revived that only recently an institution has been founded for the study of alchemy. My translations of *Jatakaparijata* <note>A classic 15th-century Sanskrit text on astrology</note>, *Sripatipaddhati* <note>A foundational work on calculating planetary strengths</note> and *Brihatjataka* <note>One of the five main treatises of Hindu astrology by Varahamihira</note> have been appreciatingly received by the public. People who started scoffing have stayed on to exhibit a living interest and to learn what the science of astrology has to teach mankind. An appreciable number of predictions by Cheiro <note>William John Warner (1866–1936), a famous Irish astrologer and palmist</note>, whose recent demise we all deplore, based upon his study of this ancient, sacred and useful system has come true. For the service of humanity, the value of astrological publications cannot be gainsaid and no apology is needed by the author for a rendering of "*Phaladeepika*" <note>Literally "Light on Fruits (Results)"; a major 16th-century work on predictive astrology</note> and placing it before the public in an English garb for the first time with suitable explanations.

"*Phaladeepika*," a fascinating study in the technique of Astrology, was so far available only in <term>Grantha</term> <note>An ancient script used in South India for writing Sanskrit</note> characters and very lately in the <term>Devanagari</term> <note>The standard script used for modern Hindi and Sanskrit</note> script. Existing publications of *Phaladeepika* are incomplete and wanting in some chapters with some <term>slokas</term> <note>Verses or poetic lines</note> in the text also mismatched. Attempts have now been made to present the public with a complete text. A great deal of difficulty was experienced in tracing the XXVIII <term>Adhyaya</term> <note>Chapter</note> which was so far unavailable and in locating a few verses in their proper places in the text.

Mantreswara, the author of this important work, whose family deity was Sukuntalamba, came from the Tinnevelly District, the Southern end of the Indian Peninsula, and he appears to have flourished about the 16th century of the Christian Era, to judge from the intrinsic evidence available.

<insert>p ?</insert>

<summary>The author introduces his English translation of the Phaladeepika, noting the revival of interest in ancient sciences and his efforts to compile a complete version of the text from fragmented sources. He identifies the original author, Mantreswara, as a 16th-century scholar from Southern India.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the preface of the Phaladeepika, detailing the editorial improvements and acknowledgments by the translator, V. Subrahmanya Sastri.</meta>

<page-num>iv</page-num>
-># PHALADEEPIKA<-

from certain <term>slokas</term><note>Sanskrit verses or stanzas</note> quoted from the *Jatakaparijata* <note>A classic 15th-century Sanskrit text on astrology</note>. His treatment of Astrology is remarkable in many ways and differs from that of several others. Mantreswara gives a very reasonable exposition of the effects of Transits and of cause and effect flowing from such Transits. We are also indebted to him for the useful information and guidance which he furnishes concerning the effect of <term>Bhavas</term><note>Astrological houses representing different areas of life</note>. His chapter on <term>Yogas</term><note>Specific planetary combinations that produce particular results</note> is an interesting study of truth. The <term>Sarvatobhadrachakra</term><note>A complex "all-auspicious" chart used for predicting events and analyzing the influence of transits</note> which was referred to only by name in other publications has been very fully explained here.

The sixteenth verse in <term>Adhyaya</term><note>Chapter</note> III which has been till now appearing with two different metres for the former and latter halves finds corrected here. The last verse in the XXXI Chapter which was marked for incompleteness is now printed in its entirety. An index in both English and Sanskrit is also added for easy reference.

I should take this opportunity of expressing my gratitude to Mr. A. S. Sowmyanarayanaiengar of Srirangam (Trichinopoly) for his ready and willing assistance in tracing and supplying some of the missing verses and also the XXVIII chapter. I am thankful to Mr. V. B. Sreekantiah, Proprietor of Messrs. V. B. Soobbiah & Sons for expediting the publication and for his personal attention to the details of printing without which it would have been impossible to undertake or complete this arduous task satisfactorily.

50 3rd Cross Road }
  Basavangudi     }
  Bangalore City  }
6th January 193<note>The final digit of the year is illegible, likely 1937.</note> }

15--16. The portions of human dwellings or habitats signified by the planets. The points of the compass over which the planets hold lordship.

17--20. Additional information (beyond what is contained in verses 1--7 *above*),—occupations, beasts, and birds appropriate to each—that can be gathered through the various planets.

21--22. The natural friends and enemies of the several planets are listed.

23. The temporary mutual relationships among the planets <note>In Vedic astrology, planets have "natural" relationships and "temporary" ones based on their specific positions in a birth chart</note>.

24. The time periods signified by the planets. The castes of the planets. Their division into <term>Sattva (Purity/Light)</term>, <term>Rajas (Passion/Activity)</term>, and <term>Tamas (Ignorance/Inertia)</term>. The seasons they represent.

25. Two pairs of planets playing the role of parents during the day and night respectively. The nearest relations signified by Mars, Jupiter, and Mercury.

26. The body and soul of a person are signified by the Moon and Sun respectively. The five senses and the planets representing them.

27. Planets—benefic <note>auspicious or favorable</note> and malefic <note>inauspicious or harmful</note>—male, female, and hermaphrodite. The deities presiding over them. The elements which they control.

28. The grains appropriate to the planets. The countries signified by them.

29. The precious stones ascribed to them.

30. Substances pertaining to the planets. Their articles of clothing.

31. The flavors signified by the planets.

32. Parts of the body where the characteristic marks or moles of the planets are to be found. The stages of human life typified by the planets.

33--34. <unclear>The effects of</unclear> Rahu and Ketu <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon, often called "shadow planets"</note> upon the character and appearance of the person born under their influence.

35. Their appropriate metals and apparel. Their friends and enemies.

### Chapter 3. <note>original: "Adhyaya"</note>

1. The ten-fold divisions of signs. <note>original: "Dasavargas"</note> The best portion of a sign. <note>original: "Vargottamamsa"</note>
2. The seven-fold divisions. The six-fold divisions. The effect of a ninth-part division <note>original: "Navamsa"</note> is equal to that of the whole sign. <note>original: "Rasi"</note>
3. The extent of the effect of planets in the whole sign and the other divisional charts. <note>original: "Vargas"</note> The five states or conditions of a planet, measured by its progress in a sign.
4. The half-sign divisions <note>original: "Horas"</note> and their lords—The ten-degree divisions <note>original: "Decanates"</note> and their lords. The twelfth-part divisions <note>original: "Dwadasamsas"</note> and their lords. The degree-based divisions <note>original: "Trimsamsas"</note> and their lords. The ninth-part divisions.
5. The sixty-part divisions. <note>original: "Shashtyamsas"</note>
6. The seven-part divisions. The ten-part divisions. The sixteen-part divisions and their lords. 7, 10 and 13 auspicious divisions.
7. Special planetary distinctions. <note>original: "Vaiseshikamsas"</note>
8—9. Their effects.
10. Effects of planets being weak or strong in all or any of the 10 divisions. Effect of a planet being in the five states mentioned in verse <note>original: "sloka"</note> 3 above.
11. Effect of planets strong in the six-fold divisions.
12. Effect of planets occupying the 2 half-sign divisions.
13. Weapon-bearing <note>original: "Ayudha"</note>, Noose <note>original: "Pasa"</note>, Fetter <note>original: "Nigala"</note> and Bird <note>original: "Pakshi"</note> ten-degree divisions explained.
14. Four-footed <note>original: "Chatushpada"</note> or quadruped ten-degree divisions. Their effects. Which of the three divisions of a sign will be good, bad, or ordinary and in what signs?
15. Effect of planets owning the rising division <note>original: "Lagna decanate"</note>, rising half-sign, rising twelfth-part and rising degree-division occupying his own, exaltation or friendly house.
16. A clue to find the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note> from the position of the mathematical point Mandi at a birth or from the Moon himself if the Moon be strong.

### Chapter 4 <note>original: "Adhyaya 4"</note>

1 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; The six kinds of strength <note>original: "Shadbalas"</note> enumerated: &nbsp; **Strength based on the three parts of day and night** <note>original: "Dinaratri tribhagabala"</note>, **Strength of the lunar fortnight** <note>original: "Pakshabala"</note>, **Strength of the Lord of the Year** <note>original: "Varshapabala"</note>, **Strength of the Lord of the Month** <note>original: "Masapabala"</note>, **Strength of the Lord of the Day** <note>original: "Dinapabala"</note>, **Strength of the Lord of the Hour** <note>original: "Horapabala"</note>

2 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; **Motional strength** <note>original: "Cheshtabala"</note>, **Strength from planetary war** <note>original: "Yuddhabala"</note>, **Strength from exaltation** <note>original: "Uchchabala"</note>, **Directional strength** <note>original: "Digbala"</note>

3 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; **Strength born of divisional positions** <note>original: "Vargajabala"</note>, **Strength of the angles and other houses** <note>original: "Kendradibala"</note>, **Strength of the decanate** <note>original: "Dreshkanabala"</note>, **Inherent or natural strength** <note>original: "Nisargabala"</note>.

4—5 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; When is a planet said to be strong and when weak?

6 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; **Strength of the astrological houses** <note>original: "Bhavabala"</note>

7 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Extent of strength accrued to planets through their position in exaltation, **Root-Trine** <note>original: "Moolatrikona"; a specific favorable area of a sign</note>, **Own sign** <note>original: "Swakshetra"</note>, friendly and other houses.

8 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Strength gained by planets in the four **Angles** <note>original: "Kendras"; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>.

9 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Importance of the 7th house aspect.

10 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Of the **Natural** <note>original: "Nisarga"</note> and **Temporary** <note>original: "Tatkalika"</note> friendships, the former is more preferable.

11 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; In warding off evil and promoting prosperity, Jupiter, Mercury and Venus compared. &nbsp; Moon is the basis for the strength of all planets.

12—20. **Lunar activity** <note>original: "Chandrakriya"</note>, **Lunar state** <note>original: "Chandravasttha"</note>, and **Lunar time/position** <note>original: "Chandravela"</note> and their effects.

21 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; **Fortnightly strength** <note>original: "Pakshabala"</note>, special to the Moon, and **Positional strength** <note>original: "Sthana bala"</note>, important to the other planets.

22—23. The standards of the **Sixfold Strengths** <note>original: "Shadbalas"</note> of planets.

24 &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; **House strength** <note>original: "Bhavabala"</note> how computed?

17. The conditions that determine the full strength or weakness of the favorable and unfavorable effects described above.

# CONTENTS

33. Effects of the transits of Saturn, the Sun, Mars and Jupiter over the 12th, the 8th and the 1st houses reckoned from the Moon's place.

35. The distribution of the 27 stars over the several limbs of the native during the Sun's transit and the effects thereof.
36—40. The same with reference to the transits of the Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, <term>Rahu</term> <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> and <term>Ketu</term> <note>The South Node of the Moon</note>.
41. Effect of transits by planets over houses containing more benefic dots in the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A system of "eight-fold points" used to calculate the strength of planetary transits</note>.
42—44. Forward <term>Lattas</term> <note>original: "पुरोलत्ता" (Puro Lattas); an astrological "kick" or obstruction caused by a planet's movement</note> and rear <term>Lattas</term> <note>original: "पृष्ठलत्ता" (Prishta Lattas)</note>.
45—46. Effects of the <term>Lattas</term>.
47. Conjunction of two or more <term>Lattas</term> and its effects.
48. 49. Expiation by <term>Propitiatory Rites</term> <note>original: "Santhis"; rituals performed to calm or appease planetary influences</note> of evil effects arising from bad <term>Major Periods</term> <note>original: "Dasas"</note>, <term>Sub-periods</term> <note>original: "Apaharas"</note>, low figure of benefic dots, transit through bad places, etc.
50. Planets are always productive of good to persons who do not harm others, do virtuous acts, etc.

<meta>This page marks the beginning of the Phaladeepika, a classic 16th-century Sanskrit treatise on predictive astrology. The translation maintains the formal, scholarly tone of the original English commentary while clarifying technical astronomical terms.</meta>

->Salutations to the Lord Ganesha<-

# PHALADEEPIKA
## LIGHT ON FRUITS (OF ASTROLOGY)

---

### CHAPTER I

->That which grants sight to the ancestors, the gods, and men,<-
->By months, years, and half-days, moving upward;<-
->Moving sometimes to the right and sometimes to the left,<-
->May that supreme Light grant us immeasurable prosperity. || 1 ||<-

### Chapter I.

> *Verse 1.* May we be blessed with beatitude without end by that Supreme Reality — the one heavenly light without a second <note>original: "Suryadi" - referring here to the Sun as the primary light</note>, which, when once above the horizon, remains on end in full view of the moon-dwelling ancestors <note>manes: the spirits of the dead</note>, pole-dwelling celestials <note>the gods residing at the North Pole</note> and the earth-dwelling mortals, for (i.e., which has for these beings a diurnal arc measuring) respectively half a lunar month, half a solar year and the entire day-time of a solar <term>nycthemeron</term> <note>a period of 24 hours consisting of one day and one night</note>, and which sometimes (to wit, with northern declination under north polar heaven) is <term>dextral</term> <note>moving from left to right</note>, and sometimes (to wit, with southern declination under south polar heaven), <term>sinistral</term> <note>moving from right to left</note>.

->Having bowed with devotion to the Goddess of Speech, the family deity, my teachers who grant knowledge of the three periods of time,<-
->The nine planets beginning with the Sun, Lord Ganesha, and the Supreme Lord;<-
->The astrologer Mantreshwara, having condensed the teachings of Atri and Parasara,<-
->Shall speak this pure "Phaladeepika" for the pleasure of the astrologers. || 2 ||<-

<summary>This opening page contains the traditional invocations to Ganesha and the Sun, establishing the astronomical basis for time-keeping among different beings and stating the author Mantreshwara's intent to summarize ancient astrological wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>Phaladeepika, Mantreshwara, Ganesha, Sun, ancestors, nycthemeron, Parasara, Atri, Vedic astrology</keywords>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory invocations and begins the technical instructions for casting a horoscope, specifically focusing on the calculation of birth time and the mapping of the zodiac to the human body.</meta>

# Phaladeepika      Chapter I

*Verse 2.* I, the astrologer Mantreswara, first make my reverent obeisance to Saraswati—the Muse of Learning—to my family deity, to my parents and teachers, and to the nine planets beginning with the Sun, which have the power to grant omniscience <note>original: "tri-kala-jnana," the knowledge of past, present, and future</note>. I also bow to Ganesa, the lord of the divine hosts, and above all, to the Supreme Lord Siva. I now proceed to provide, for the delight of astrologers, a very clear summary of the teachings of the sages Atri, Parasara, and others, under the title "Phaladeepika" <note>The title translates to "Light on the Fruits of Destiny" or "Lamp of Astrology."</note>.

->First, the exact moment of a person's birth must be carefully determined,<-
->specifically down to the smallest units of time <term>vighatikas</term>,<-
->using methods such as the measurement of shadows. Then,<-
->having calculated the true positions of the planets through mathematics,<-
->one should determine the houses and planetary strengths to predict the results.<-

*Verse 3.* The exact time of the birth of a person, including the actual number of *vighatikas* <note>A vighatika is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 seconds.</note> elapsed—as revealed by foot-measurement, the shadow of a gnomon <note>An ancient sundial instrument.</note>, or other apparatus—should first be specially ascertained. Then, the positions of the planets, aligned with actual observation, should be calculated using mathematical instruments. Then the <term>bhavas</term> <note>Astrological houses representing different areas of life.</note> and the strengths of the planets should be investigated, and through them, the effects should be judged.

->The head, face, chest, stomach, waist, pelvis, and genitals,<-
->followed by the two thighs, the two knees, the two shins, and the two feet.<-
->These are the limbs of the Time-Person <term>Kala</term> starting from the Ascendant,<-
->corresponding to the signs from Aries to Pisces; this is the well-known view.<-

*Verse 4.* The parts of the body of the personified Time <term>Kala</term> <note>The concept of the zodiac as a cosmic human body.</note> beginning with the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth.</note> are respectively: (1) the

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>The author Mantreswara invokes divine blessings and outlines the essential preliminary steps for an astrological reading, emphasizing the precise measurement of time and the correlation between zodiac signs and parts of the human body.</summary>

*Verse 5.* The abodes of the 12 signs from <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note> onwards are respectively (1) the forest (2) a field under water (meadow) (3) a bed room (4) a chasm with water in it (5) a mountain (6) a land full of water and corn (7) the house of a <term>Vaisya</term> <note>The merchant or agriculturalist class in traditional Indian society</note> (8) a hole or cavity (9) King's residence (10) water-abounding forest (11) the spot frequented by potters and (12) water.

->Saturn, and Jupiter are declared the lords of the signs from Aries in order.<-

->At degrees ten, three, twenty-eight, fifteen, five, and twenty, they reach their highest and lowest points.<-

*Verse 6.* Mars, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, the Sun, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Saturn and Jupiter are respectively declared the lords of the signs from <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note> onwards. <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note>, <term>Vrishabha</term> <note>Taurus</note>, <term>Makara</term> <note>Capricorn</note>, <term>Kanya</term> <note>Virgo</note>, <term>Kataka</term> <note>Cancer</note>, <term>Meena</term> <note>Pisces</note> and <term>Tula</term> <note>Libra</note> are the <term>exaltation</term> <note>The sign where a planet's influence is most powerful</note> signs of the seven planets respectively from the Sun onwards, their signs of <term>fall</term> <note>The sign where a planet's influence is weakest, also called 'debilitation'</note> being the 7th from their exaltation ones. ->Leo, Taurus, Aries, Virgo, Sagittarius, Libra, and Aquarius are the primary triangular signs of the Sun and others.<-
->The exaltation degrees of the Moon and others are 3, 20, 15, 10, 5, and 20.<-

<note>original: "सिंहोक्षजवधूयहाङ्गरणजः कुंभस्त्रिकोणा रवेः..." This verse categorizes the zodiac signs by their elemental nature (human, animal, or water) and defines the specific degrees where planets are most powerful.</note>

*Verse 7.* Leo, Taurus, Aries, Virgo, Sagittarius, Libra and Aquarius are the <term>Moolatrikona</term> (primary triangular/favorable) signs of the planets from the Sun onwards. The first 20 degrees of Leo, the last 27 degrees of Taurus (27 degrees after the highest exaltation degree), the first 12 degrees of Aries, the 5 degrees following the highest exaltation degree of Mercury in Virgo (*i.e.*, 16° to 20°), the first 10 degrees in Sagittarius, the first five degrees in Libra and the first 20 degrees of Aquarius form respectively the <term>Moolatrikona</term> portions of the 7 planets from the Sun onwards. The first half of Sagittarius, Virgo, Gemini, Aquarius and Libra are bipeds or human signs. Scorpio is a <term>Keeta</term> (reptile or centipede) sign. Cancer, the latter half of Capricorn and Pisces are watery signs. The rest, *viz.*, Aries, Taurus, Leo, Sagittarius (latter half) and Capricorn (first half) are quadruped (four-legged) signs.

->Taurus, Cancer, Aries, and Capricorn rise from the back; Gemini and Pisces are the others.<-
->These rise from the back or both ways; Gemini and the Moon's signs rise from the back.<-
->The Sun's signs and the rest are described in order as night and day signs.<-
->Upward, downward, and level are the directions from the house vacated by the Sun. || 8 ||<-
<note>original: "गोकर्क्स्यव्यजनक्रमान्यथ नृयुङ्मीनो परे राशय..." This verse explains the "rising" orientation of the signs—whether they appear to emerge from the horizon head-first or tail-first—which affects how their influence is interpreted.</note>

*Verse 8.* The signs Taurus, Cancer, Sagittarius, Aries and Capricorn rise with their backs (<term>Prishtodaya</term>—rising from the rear). Gemini and Pisces come under <term>Ubhayodaya</term> (rising both ways).

<page-num>5</page-num>
->Sl. 8-10 &emsp; &emsp; &emsp; Chapter One &emsp; &emsp; &emsp; 5<-

(rising both ways <note>original: "ubhayodaya"</note>). The rest appear with their faces and are termed head-rising <note>original: "Sirshodaya"</note> signs. The back-rising <note>original: "Prishtodaya"</note> signs and Gemini <note>original: "Mithuna"</note> belong to the Moon and are termed nocturnal signs <note>original: "Rasis"</note>. The other six belong to the Sun and are termed diurnal signs. The four signs counted from the sign last passed (left) by the Sun are in their order termed (1) upward <note>original: "Urdhva"</note> or tending upwards (2) downward <note>original: "Adhah"</note>—beneath or under (3) even <note>original: "Sama"</note>—even or level and (4) crooked <note>original: "Vakra"</note>—bent or crooked. The same order holds good in the case of the other 8 signs.

The signs starting from Aries are moveable, fixed, and dual; entrance, exterior, and interior;
&emsp; Mineral, vegetable, and animal; likewise, they are known as fierce and auspicious.
Aries and the others, along with their triangular counterparts, are lords of the East and other directions in order;
&emsp; Odd and even signs are male and female, representing the left and right limbs. || 9 ||

**Verse 9.** The signs from Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> taken in order are (1) moveable <note>original: "Chara"</note>—cardinal, fixed <note>original: "Sthira"</note> and dual <note>original: "Ubhaya"</note>—mutable or common; (2) entrance <note>original: "Dwara"</note>, open <note>original: "Bahis"</note>—outside and inside <note>original: "Garbha"</note>; (3) mineral <note>original: "Dhatu"</note>, vegetable <note>original: "Mula"</note> and animal <note>original: "Jeeva"</note>; (4) fierce <note>original: "Krura"</note> and auspicious <note>original: "Saumya"</note>; (5) odd and even and (6) male and female. Aries, Taurus, Gemini, and Cancer <note>original: "Mesha, Vrishabha, Mithuna and Kataka"</note> with their triangular <note>original: "Trikona"</note> signs represent the four quarters commencing from the East. The six houses from the 7th represent the left-side limbs of the Cosmic Man <note>original: "Kalapurusha"</note> while the other six houses (i.e., reckoned from the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note>) represent the right-side ones.

The Ascendant is called the Hour, the Body, the Rise, the Form, the Head, the Present, and Birth.
Wealth, Knowledge, Food and Drink, Enjoyment, the Right Eye, and the Document... || 10 ||

# 8 Phaladeepika Chapter I

**Commerce** <note>original: "Vyapara"</note>, **Rank or Position** <note>original: "Aspada"</note>, **Honor** <note>original: "Mana"</note>, **Occupation** <note>original: "Karma"</note>, **Success** <note>original: "Jaya"</note>, **Goodness** <note>original: "Sat"</note>, **Fame** <note>original: "Kirti"</note>, **Sacrifice** <note>original: "Kratu"</note>, **Livelihood or Profession** <note>original: "Jeevana"</note>, **Sky or Zenith** <note>original: "Vyoma"</note>, **Good Conduct** <note>original: "Achara"</note>, **Quality** <note>original: "Guna"</note>, **Inclination** <note>original: "Pravritti"</note>, **Gait** <note>original: "Gamana"</note>, **Command** <note>original: "Ajna"</note>, and **Mid-heaven** <note>original: "Meshurana", a Greek-derived term 'mesouranema' used in Sanskrit astrology</note>, **Gain** <note>original: "Labha"</note>, **Income** <note>original: "Aya"</note>, **Acquisition** <note>original: "Agamana"</note>, **Getting or Gain** <note>original: "Apthi"</note>, **Accomplishment or Fulfillment** <note>original: "Siddhi"</note>, **Wealth or Riches** <note>original: "Vibhava"</note>, **Profit** <note>original: "Prapthi"</note>, **Being or Existence** <note>original: "Bhava"</note>, **Veneration or Commendation** <note>original: "Slaghyata"</note>, **eldest brother or sister**, **left ear**, **anything juicy or succulent** <note>original: "Sarasa"</note>, and (hearing of some) **pleasing or delightful news** are the expressions for the **11th house**.

---

->Misery, leg, left eye, loss, spy,<-
->last, poverty, sin, bed, expenditure,<-
->the word 'Ripha', and imprisonment—<-
->these are the names declared in order. Then, the<-
->'Hidden Places' are the 3rd, 6th, 12th, and 8th houses.<-

**Verse 16:** The designations used for the **12th house** are **Misery** <note>original: "Duhkha"</note>, **Leg** <note>original: "Anghri"</note>, **Left Eye** <note>original: "Vama Nayana"</note>, **Loss or Decline** <note>original: "Kshaya"</note>, **A Tale-bearer or Spy** <note>original: "Suchaka"</note>, **Last** <note>original: "Anthya"</note>, **Poverty** <note>original: "Daridrya"</note>, **Sin** <note>original: "Papa"</note>, **Bed** <note>original: "Sayana"</note>, **Expenditure** <note>original: "Vyaya"</note>, **The Low** <note>original: "Ripha"</note>, and **Imprisonment** <note>original: "Bandha"</note>. Thus have been declared in their order the names of the 12 houses. The 3rd, the 6th, the 12th, and the 8th houses are termed **Hidden Houses** <note>original: "Leena Sthanas"</note>.

->The 8th, 6th, and 12th houses are called 'Bad Houses'; the other houses are called 'Good Houses' and are said to give auspicious results.<-
<margin>-</margin> ->The 1st, 10th, 7th, and 4th houses are called the 'Center', also known by the names 'Thorny Point' and 'The Fourfold'.<-

<note>The translator here defines the major house groups: Dusthanas (unfortunate houses), Susthanas (fortunate houses), and Kendras (the pivotal angular houses).</note>

<meta>This page continues the classification of astrological houses from the previous section and begins the second chapter focusing on the characteristics of the Sun.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>
<header>Verses 17-18    Chapter Two    9</header>

*Verse 17* The 8th, the 6th, and the 12th houses are known as *Dusstthanas* <note>original: "दु: स्थान" - literally 'bad places' or 'malefic houses'</note> or houses of evil. The rest are termed good houses and will be propitious to the native. The 1st, the 10th, the 7th, and the 4th houses are known by the terms Kendra <note>original: "केन्द्र" - angular houses</note>, Kantaka <note>original: "कण्टक" - literally 'thorn'</note>, and Chatushtaya <note>original: "चतुष्टय" - a group of four</note>.

->The houses following the Kendras are Panapharas; those beyond<-
->them are Apoklimas. The 8th and 4th are Chaturasra.<-
->The 10th, 3rd, 6th, and 11th are the Upachayas.<-
->The pair of the 9th and 5th houses are the highly praised Trikonas. || 18 ||<-
<note>This is a translation of the Sanskrit verse provided in the center of the page.</note>

->|| Thus ends the first Chapter on "Definitions" ||<-
->|| in the work Phaladeepika <note>The Light of Fruits/Results</note> composed by Mantreswara ||<-

*Verse 18* The houses next to the Kendras, i.e., the 2nd, the 5th, the 8th, and the 11th, are known as Panapharas <note>original: "पणफर" - succedent houses</note>. The 3rd, the 6th, the 9th, and the 12th are Apoklima <note>original: "आपोक्लिम" - cadent houses</note> houses. The 4th and the 8th are designated Chaturasra <note>original: "चतुरस्त्र" - quadrangular</note> houses. The 10th, the 3rd, the 6th, and the 11th houses are called Upachaya <note>original: "उपचय" - houses of growth or improvement</note>, the 9th and the 5th are known as Trikona <note>original: "त्रिकोण" - triangular or trine</note> houses and these are auspicious.

->Thus ends the first Chapter on "Definitions"<-
->in the work Phaladeepika composed by Mantreswara<-

---

## ## Chapter Two ##

->Copper, gold, father, auspicious results, the self, strength, and valor;<-
->Courage, heroism, intellect, victory, service to the king, and fame;<-
->Mountains, deeds, wandering in forests and hills, and inclination toward fire-rituals;<-
->Temples and sharpness—let the wise man declare all these from the Sun. || 1 ||<-

# Phaladeepika, Chapter II

## Chapter II

*Verse 1.* It is through the Sun that a wise man ought to ascertain about a person's copper, gold, father, anything auspicious, one's own self, happiness, dignity, courage, power, victory in war, service under the sovereign, glory, any work relating to the God Shiva, trips to forests or mountainous regions, taking an active part in fire offerings <note>original: "Homas"</note> or sacrifices <note>original: "Yagnas"</note>, temples, mental acuteness, and exertion.

-> Mother’s welfare, peace of mind, bathing in the sea, white fly-whisks <note>original: "Chowrie"; a symbol of royalty</note>, the royal umbrella, fine fans, fruits, tenderness, flowers, corn, agriculture, fame, the acquisition of pearls, bell-metal, silver, sweet substances, milk and its derivatives, clothing, water, cows, women, pleasant meals, bodily health, and beauty should all be predicted from the Moon. <-

*Verse 2.* It is from the Moon that one ought to determine the welfare of the mother, mental tranquillity, sea bath, white fly-whisk, umbrella, good fan, fruits, tenderness, flowers, corn, agriculture, fame, acquisition of pearls, bell metal, silver, sweet substances, milk, etc., cloth, water, cows, women, good meals, bodily health and beauty.

-> Strength, the fruits of the earth, the qualities of brothers, cruelty, battle, daring, hatred of enemies, the kitchen <note>original: "Mahanasa"</note>, fire, gold, kinsmen, weapons, thieves, enemies, enthusiasm, lusting after other men's wives, falsehood, prowess, loftiness of thought, sin, leadership of an army, and wounds are to be told from the Son of the Earth <note>original: "Mahija," a name for Mars</note>. <-

*Verse 3.* It is through Mars that a person should ascertain his strength, products derived from the Earth, the qualities of his brothers, cruelty, battle, daring courage, odium, cooking utensils, fire, gold, kindred, weapon, thieves, enemies, energy, attachment to females not his own, uttering falsehood, prowess, mental dignity (loftiness of thought), sin, commanding an army, and wounds.

->Scholarship, eloquence, skill in the arts, praise from the wise, the maternal uncle,<-

*Verse 4.* It is Mercury that influences one’s learning; eloquence, skill in the fine arts <term>fine arts: "Kala-nipuna," referring to the 64 traditional Indian arts</term>, eulogy from the learned, maternal uncle, dexterity in speech, cleverness in religious meditation and the like, aptness for acquiring knowledge, sacrifice, any religious rite relating to Vishnu, truth-speaking, oyster-shell, place of recreation, skill in mechanical arts, relations, the rank of an heir-apparent, friends and one's sister's son or daughter, etc.

->Wisdom, virtuous qualities, children, a minister, good conduct, the role of a teacher,<-

->devotion to Gods and Brahmins, sacrifices, penance, faith, the treasury,<-
->erudition, mastery over the senses, the happiness of a husband, honor, and compassion—all these are from Jupiter. <note>original: "Ijya"</note> ॥ 5 ॥<-

*Verse 5.* It is through Jupiter that one ought to seek information about one’s knowledge, good qualities, sons, minister, behaviour, teaching, magnanimity, knowledge of <term>Vedas, Sastras and Smritis: the primary layers of Hindu sacred literature, law, and tradition</term>, prosperity in everything, beatitude, reverence to Gods and Brahmins, sacrifice, penance, religious faith, treasure-house, wisdom (learning), conquering of the senses, happiness of the husband, honour and compassion.

->Wealth, vehicles, clothing, ornaments, hidden treasures, the triple arts of music,<-
->happiness from a wife, perfumes, flowers, sexual union, beds, and houses,<-
->splendor, the joy of poetry, association with many women, grace, passion,<-
->ministerial office, witty speech, and the celebration of marriage—these are said to come from Venus. <note>original: "Bhriguja"</note> ॥ 6॥<-

*Verse 6.* Wealth, vehicles, clothes, ornaments, treasures, the triple arts <note>original: "Tauryatrikam," the combined arts of singing, instrumental music, and dance</note>, happiness from a wife, perfumes, flowers, amorous activities, beds, and dwellings; splendor, the pleasure of poetry, association with many women, charm, pride, ministerial work, elegant speech, and the festivities of marriage are all attributed to Venus.

# Phaladeepika Chapter II

*Verse 6. Information regarding one's wealth, vehicles, clothes, ornaments, hoarded goods, the triple symphony <note>original: "sangita" - the traditional union of vocal music, dance, and instrumental performance</note>, wife, happiness, scents, flowers, sexual intercourse, couches, temples, prosperity, poetry, attraction to many maidens, sport, lasciviousness, ministerial office, charming speech, marriage, and other celebrations should be sought through Venus.*

->Longevity, death, fear, degradation, misery, humiliation, and sickness,<-
->Poverty, labor for hire, reproach, impurity, and states of censure,<-
->Constancy, seeking refuge with the lowly, buffaloes, drowsiness, debt, and iron,<-
->Servitude, agricultural tools, imprisonment, and captivity are from the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>. || 7 ||<-

*Verse 7. As regards one's longevity, death, fear, degradation, misery, humiliation, sickness, poverty, manual labor, reproach, sin, impurity, censure, misfortune, constancy, resorting to low people, buffaloes, drowsiness, debts, iron, servitude, agricultural implements, jail, and captivity, one ought to discern through Saturn.*

->The Sun is of a bilious nature, strong in the bones, with little hair, and a dark-red complexion; his eyes are the color of honey.<-
->Clad in saffron-dyed garments, with a square-built body, he is valiant, fierce, and broad-shouldered. || 8 ||<-

*Verse 8. The Sun is of a bilious temperament <term>Pitta: one of the three humors in Ayurvedic medicine associated with fire and heat</term> and is the lord of the bones in the body. He has a limited quantity of hair and possesses a dark-red form. He has eyes of a reddish-brown color. He is clad in red and has a square-built body. He is valiant and wrathful, and has broad shoulders.*

->The Moon is bulky, both young and old, lean, white, with beautiful eyes and fine black hair.<-
->Strong in the blood, soft-spoken, wearing white garments, fair-skinned, of a windy and phlegmatic nature, and gentle.<-

*Verse 9. The Moon has a huge body and is young as well as old <note>This refers to the waxing and waning phases of the Moon</note>; he is lean and white; has fine lovely eyes,*

black and thin hair. He governs blood. He is soft in speech, wears a white garment and is yellowish in colour. He has wind and phlegm in his composition and is mild in temperament. <note>This concluding sentence refers to the Moon, described on the preceding page.</note>

->Mars is slender at the waist, with curled and shining hair; he has fierce eyes, a bilious nature, and a sharp intellect.<-

**Verse 10.** Mars has a slender waist, curled and shining hair. He has fierce eyes; he is bilious <note>original: "paittika," referring to the fire-based humor</note> and cruel in nature. He wears a red garment, and is of a reddish hue. He is wrathful but exceedingly generous. He has a youthful appearance and governs the marrow of the bones and flesh.

->His body is dark green like a blade of Durva grass; he is a mixture of the three humors, with visible veins and sweet speech.<-
->With long reddish eyes, dressed in green, Mercury governs the skin, loves laughter, and has a well-proportioned body.<-

**Verse 11.** Mercury is green as the blade of Durva or panic grass. He is a mixture of the three humours <term>Vata</term> (wind), <term>Pitta</term> (bile) and <term>Kapha</term> (phlegm). His body is full of nerves. He is pleasant in his speech. He has red and broad eyes and is clad in green. He governs the skin, is fond of fun and possesses even limbs.

->He possesses a golden luster, with reddish-brown hair and eyes; he has a thick, elevated chest and a large body.<-
->Of a phlegmatic nature, with superior intelligence, the Teacher of the Gods has a voice like a lion or a conch and is focused on wealth.<-

**Verse 12.** Jupiter has a body of yellowish hue. His eyes and hair are brown. He has got a fat and elevated chest and possesses a big body. He is phlegmatic in temperament and is exceedingly intelligent. His voice is similar to that of a lion or the sound of the conch. He is always after wealth.

If <term>vasapradhanah</term> <note>original: "वसाप्रधानः"</note> be the reading, the translation will be, “He governs fat.”

# Phaladeepika Chapter II

---

> <note>original: "citrāmbara-kuñcita-kṛṣṇa-keśaḥ sthūlāṅga-dehaśca kaphānilātmā | dūrvāṅkurābhaḥ kamano viśālanetro bhṛguḥ sādhita-śukla-vṛddiḥ || 13 ||"</note>

*Verse 13.* **Venus** <note>referred to here as Bhrigu</note> is dressed in garments of variegated colors. He has black, curly hair. His limbs and body are large, and his physical constitution is dominated by the humors of phlegm and wind <note>In the Ayurvedic system, these are Kapha and Vata</note>. His complexion is the color of a blade of *Durva* grass <note>a sacred green grass used in rituals</note>. He is very handsome and possesses broad, attractive eyes. He is characterized by an abundance of vital reproductive energy.

> <note>original: "paṅgur-nimna-villocanaḥ kṛśatanur-dīrghaḥ sirālo'lasaḥ kṛṣṇāṅgaḥ pavanātmakogati-piśunaḥ snāyvātmano nirguṇaḥ | mūrkhaḥ sthūlanakhadvijaḥ paruṣaromāṅgo'śucistāmaso raudraḥ krodhaparo jarāpariṇataḥ kṛṣṇāmbaro bhāskariḥ || 14 ||"</note>

*Verse 14.* **Saturn** <note>referred to here as Bhaskari, the son of the Sun</note> is dark in form and lame in his gait. He is of a windy temperament <note>Vata</note>. He has deep-set eyes and a lean, tall body marked by visible veins and arteries. He is prone to laziness and is known for being slanderous. He rules over the tendons and muscles of the body. He is harsh, lacking in virtue, and dull-witted. He possesses large nails and teeth, with stiff hair and a rough body. He is unclean, governed by the quality of darkness <note>Tamas</note>, fierce, and easily moved to anger. He appears as one ripened by old age and wears black clothing.

> <note>original: "śaivaṃ dhāma bahiḥprakāśarumuruddeśo raveḥ pūrvadik durgāsthānamadhūjalaupadhimadhusthānaṃ nidhorvāyudik | coramlecchakṛśānuyuddhabhuvi diggāmyā kujasyoditā vidvadvidhuṣasabhāvihāragaṇakasthānāpyudīcīṃ viduḥ || 15 ||"</note>

*Verse 15.* The following are the places associated with the **Sun** <note>Ravi</note>: a temple dedicated to Shiva, an open area, a place illuminated by bright light, a region lacking water, and the Eastern direction. The **Moon's** <note>Vidhu</note> abodes are the temple of the Goddess Durga, places where honey or water is found, and the Northwest direction. For **Mars**, the places are those frequented by thieves, foreign lands <note>original: mleccha</note>, areas near fire, battlefields, and the Southern direction. For **Mercury**, the places are those where learned men gather, temples of Vishnu, assembly halls, recreation grounds, and the offices of mathematicians or astrologers; his direction is the North.

# Chapter Two
<meta>Verses 16-17</meta>

The place where Goddess Parvati is kept <note>original: "Devi"; likely referring to a shrine or temple dedicated to the Divine Mother</note>, the spot occupied by a female, a place where there is water, a herb or plant, a spot where honey or liquor is kept, and the North-west quarter. The places resorted to by thieves, or occupied by low people, one where there is fire, a battle-field, and the Southern quarter are declared to belong to Mars. Mercury governs the places frequented by learned men, the spot where God Vishnu is kept, an assembly, a recreation ground, a mathematical hall, and the Northern quarter.

->The treasury, the sacred fig tree, the dwellings of gods and the twice-born are the abodes of Jupiter in the North-east;<-
->The quarters of courtesans, the wealthy, the harem, the dancing hall, and the bedroom belong to Venus in the South-east;<-
->The streets of the lowly, impure places, and the temple of the Holy Guide belong to Saturn in the West;<-
->Ant-hills and the dark holes of serpents are the places of the Dragon's Head and Tail according to their directions.<-

**Verse 16** The places belonging to Jupiter are: Treasury, the <term>Aswattha</term> <note>The Ficus religiosa or Sacred Fig tree, considered holy in India</note>, the dwellings of Gods and Brahmins <note>original: "dvija" or twice-born</note>, and the North-east quarter. The places of Venus are courtesans’ quarters, the harem, the dancing hall, the bedroom, and the South-east quarter. Saturn’s abodes are the lines where low people live, dirty places, the Western quarter, and the temple of the deity <term>Sasta</term> <note>A guardian deity often associated with the wilderness and protection</note> (generally known as <gloss>Aiyanar Temple</gloss> in Tamil and analogous to <gloss>Muneshwara Temple</gloss> in Kannada). The places of Rahu and Ketu <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon, often called the Dragon's Head and Tail</note> are an ant-hill, the dark holes occupied by serpents, and the South-western direction.

->A worshipper of Shiva, a physician, a king, a weaver, and a minister;<-
->The tiger, the deer, and the ruddy sheldrake belong to the Lord of Day;<-
->The Holy Guide, women, washermen, crabs, and water-dwellers belong to the Moon;<-
->As do the rabbit, the antelope, the crane, and the Greek partridge.<-

**Verse 17** A worshipper of Shiva, a physician, a <insert>king, a cloth-maker, a minister, a tiger, a deer, and the Chakravaka bird belong to the Sun.</insert> The deity Sasta, women, washermen, the crab, and those who live by water belong to the Moon; as do the rabbit, the deer, the crane, and the <term>Chakora</term> <note>A legendary bird said to live on moonbeams</note>.

A king, a performer of a sacrifice, a minister, a tiger, a deer, and the ruddy goose, are signified by the Sun. One who worships <term>Sasta</term> <note>A deity often identified with Ayyappa or a guardian spirit</note>, a female, a washerman, a cultivator, an aquatic animal, a hare, an antelope, a crane and the Greek partridge are denoted by the Moon.

->Mars, the cook, the weapon-bearer, the goldsmith,<-
->the ram, the cock, the jackal, the monkey, the vulture, and the thief.<-
->The cowherd, the artisan, the accountant, the devotee of Vishnu,<-
->the eagle, the blue jay, the parrot, and the cat belong to Mercury.<-

*Verse 18.* Matters relating to the cook (in the kitchen), an armour-bearer, a goldsmith, a ram, a cock, a jackal, a monkey, a vulture and a thief belong to Mars. A cowherd, a learned man, an artisan, a clever accountant, a <term>Vishnu bhakta</term> <note>A devotee or worshipper of the god Vishnu</note>, a Garuda <note>The mythical eagle-mount of Vishnu</note>, a Chataka bird <note>A bird said to live only on raindrops</note>, a parrot and a cat are represented by Mercury.

->The astrologer, the minister, the teacher, the priest, the head of monks,<-
->the pigeon, the horse, and the swan belong to Jupiter.<-
->The musician, the wealthy, the gallant, the merchant, the actor, the weaver,<-
->the courtesan, the peacock, the buffalo, the parrot, and the cow belong to Venus.<-

*Verse 19.* An astrologer, a minister, a preceptor, a Brahmin, a <term>Sanyasi-chief</term> <note>A leader among those who have renounced worldly life</note>, a pigeon, a horse and a swan relate to Jupiter. About a musician, a wealthy man, a sensualist, a merchant, a dancer, a weaver, a courtesan, a peacock, a buffalo, a parrot and a cow, one ought to ascertain through Venus.

<warning>The first line of Sanskrit Verse 20 is faded and partially illegible.</warning>

->...hunters, low-born men, and those who work with wood,<-
->elephants, crows, and cuckoos belong to Saturn.<-
->Buddhists, snake-charmers, wild dogs, wolves, camels, snakes,<-
->darkness, mosquitoes, bedbugs, insects, and owls.<-

*Verse 20.* <note>The beginning of this verse typically refers to oil-pressers and hunters</note> ...hunters, artisans, and those of the lowest castes, elephants, crows, and cuckoos are governed by Saturn. Buddhists <note>or followers of non-Vedic sects</note>, snake-charmers, wild dogs, wolves, camels, snakes, darkness, mosquitoes, bedbugs, insects, and owls are also associated with these darker or outer influences.

<header>Verses 20-22 Chapter Two</header>
<sig>Pha - Di - 3</sig>

*Verse 20.* An oil monger, a servant, a vile person, a hunter, a blacksmith, an elephant, a crow, and the cuckoo — these have to be assigned to Saturn. A Buddhist <note>The original text uses 'Bauddha', referring to followers of the Buddha</note>, a snake catcher, an ass, a ram, a wolf, a camel, a serpent, a place enveloped in darkness and the like, a mosquito, a bug, an insect and an owl—all these should be declared to belong to Rahu and Ketu <note>Rahu and Ketu are the north and south nodes of the Moon, often called 'shadow planets' in Vedic astrology</note>.

->Mercury is neutral to the Sun; Saturn and Venus are his enemies.<-
->The Sun and Mercury are friends to the Moon; the others are neutral.<-
->Saturn and Venus are neutral to Mars; Mercury is his enemy.<-
->The Moon is the enemy of Mercury; Venus and the Sun are his friends. || 21 ||<-
<note>original: "saumyaḥ samo'rkajasitāvahitau kharāṃśu- rindoṛhitau ravibudhāvapare samāḥ syuḥ | bhaumasya mandabhṛgujau tu samau ripujñaḥ saumyasya śītagurarariḥ suhṛdau sitārkau || 21 ||"</note>

*Verse 21.* Mercury is the Sun's neutral, Saturn and Venus are his enemies. The Moon's friends are the Sun and Mercury; the rest are all neutrals to him. Mars' neutrals are Venus and Saturn, and Mercury is his enemy. The Moon is Mercury's enemy, Venus and the Sun are his friends.

->Venus and Mercury are enemies of Jupiter; Saturn is neutral.<-
->Jupiter and Mars are neutral to Venus; Saturn and Mercury are friends.<-
->Jupiter is neutral to Saturn; Venus and Mercury are his friends.<-
->Planets not mentioned here should be understood by their opposite relations. || 22 ||<-
<note>original: "surendriṣau kavibudhau ravijaḥ samaḥ syā- nmadhyau kaveṛgurukujau suhṛdau śanijñau | jīvaḥ samaḥ sitavidau ravijasya mitre jñeyā anuktakhacarāstu tadanyathā syuḥ || 22 ||"</note>

*Verse 22.* Venus and Mercury are the enemies of Jupiter; Saturn is neutral to him. Jupiter and Mars are neutral towards Venus. Saturn and Mercury are his friends. Jupiter is neutral towards Saturn. Venus and Mercury are his friends. In cases where certain planets have been omitted, they must be considered to fulfil the relationship that has not been mentioned <note>This refers to the natural (Naisargika) friendships where, if a planet is not listed as an enemy or neutral, it is by default a friend</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of planetary relationships and characteristics from Chapter II of the Phaladeepika, a classic text on Hindu astrology.</meta>

# Phaladeepika Chapter II

---

->**Verse 23**<-

Planets are mutually friendly for the time being when they occupy the 3rd, the 4th, the 2nd, the 10th, the 12th, and the 11th houses from one another. <note>original: "tri-sukha-sva-khantya-bhavanas" referring to these specific house positions</note> Ascertain what planets are naturally friendly, what are naturally neutral, and what are inimical, and then deduce from these two kinds of friendship <note>temporary and natural</note> those that are very friendly and those that are very inimical. Saturn casts a full glance <term>Drishti: astrological aspect or gaze</term> at the 3rd and 10th houses, Jupiter at the 5th and 9th, and Mars at the 4th and 8th. All planets cast a quarter glance at the 3rd and 10th houses, half a glance at the 5th and 9th, three-quarters of a glance at the 4th and 8th, and a full eye at the 7th.

->**Verse 24**<-

The planets from the Sun onwards sway over periods of half a year <note>the solar half-year or Ayana</note>, a <term>Muhurta: a unit of time equal to 48 minutes</term> (2 <term>Ghatikas: units of 24 minutes each</term>), a day, two months <note>a season</note>, one month, 15 days <note>a lunar fortnight or Paksha</note>, and a year respectively. Venus and Jupiter are <term>Brahmins: the priestly or scholarly class</term>; the Sun and Mars are <term>Kshatriyas: the warrior or ruling class</term>. The Moon is a <term>Vaisya: the merchant or agricultural class</term>; Mercury is the lord of the <term>Sudra: the laboring class</term> community. Saturn is the leader of the outcastes. The Moon, Jupiter, and the Sun are planets typifying <term>Sattva: purity, goodness, and harmony</term>. Venus and Mercury are essentially the planets of <term>Rajas: passion, energy, and movement</term>. Mars and Saturn own <term>Tamas: darkness, inertia, and ignorance</term> as their distinguishing characteristic. The lords of the six seasons <note>Ritus</note> reckoning from <insert>the summer season are the Sun and Mars, followed by the Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and Venus.</insert>

<summary>This page details the rules for calculating temporary planetary friendships, the specific "glances" or aspects (Drishti) each planet casts, and the classification of planets by time cycles, social classes (Varnas), and inherent qualities (Gunas).</summary>

<keywords>planetary friendship, aspects, Drishti, time units, Muhurta, Varna, social class, Gunas, Sattva, Rajas, Tamas, Phaladeepika</keywords>

<page-num>19</page-num>
<header>Verses 25-27 Chapter Two 19</header>

The lords of the seasons <note>original: "Greeshma" refers to the summer season, but here the text lists the planetary sequence for various categories</note> are (1) The Sun and Mars, (2) The Moon, (3) Mercury, (4) Jupiter, (5) Saturn and (6) Venus.

---

->The Sun and Venus are known as the father and mother for those born in the day; Saturn and the Moon are so remembered for those born at night. When these planets are positioned elsewhere, they represent the paternal uncle and maternal aunt respectively. The Moon is called the left eye and the Sun the right. Mars is the youngest brother, Jupiter the eldest brother, and the son of the Moon (Mercury) is designated as the adopted son.<-

*Verse 25.* The Sun and Venus are termed father and mother respectively of the child born in the day; Saturn and the Moon, during the night. Failing to play the role of parents, each of these pairs of planets gets the designation of paternal uncle and maternal aunt. The Moon represents the left eye, while the Sun, the right one. Mars represents the youngest brother and Jupiter, the eldest one. Mercury denotes the adopted son.

->The Moon is the body and the Sun is the soul; Mars and the others are the senses. <margin><unclear>Soul?</unclear></margin> The enemies of the Sun, along with Gulika and Ketu <note>Gulika and Ketu are "shadow planets" or mathematical points in Vedic astrology often associated with karmic obstruction</note>, are the causes of suffering to these. Mercury is smell, Venus and the Moon are taste, the Sun and Mars are sight, Jupiter is sound, and the others are designated as touch.<-

*Verse 26.* The Moon represents the body, and the Sun, the soul <term>Atman</term>. Mars and the other planets denote the five senses. The enemy of the Sun, *namely*, Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, known for eclipsing the Sun</note>, Gulika and Ketu will cause trouble to the body and the soul. Mercury governs smelling (nose), Venus and the Moon, taste (mouth); the Sun and Mars, govern sight (eyes); Jupiter, hearing (ears). The rest, *namely*, Saturn, Rahu and Ketu govern touch.

->The waning Moon, Sun, Mars, Rahu, Ketu, and Saturn are the cruel ones; Mercury is cruel when associated with them. Ketu, Mercury, and Saturn are eunuchs; the Moon, Rahu, and Venus are women; the others are men. Shiva, Parvati, Skanda, Vishnu, Brahma, Lakshmi, and Yama are the presiding deities. From the Sun onwards, the planets are of the nature of Fire, Water, Fire, Earth, Ether, Air, and Soul.<-

*Verse 27.* The waning Moon, Sun, Mars, Rahu, Ketu, and Saturn are malefic <note>original: "papa" meaning sinful or harmful in influence</note>; Mercury is also malefic when joined with them. Ketu, Mercury, and Saturn are neuter; the Moon, Rahu, and Venus are female; the others are male. Shiva, Parvati, Skanda, Vishnu, Brahma, Lakshmi, and Yama <note>The text lists the Vedic deities corresponding to each planet from the Sun to Saturn</note> are the respective deities. From the Sun onwards, the planets are composed of Fire, Water, Fire, Earth, Ether <term>Akasha</term>, Air, and the Self.

<summary>This page details the planetary correspondences for family members, the five physical senses, gender, and the elemental compositions of the celestial bodies. It establishes which planets act as "malefics" and assigns a presiding Hindu deity to each planet.</summary>
<keywords>astrological correspondences, family relations, five senses, planetary gender, Vedic deities, elements, malefic planets</keywords>

# Phaladeepika: Chapter II

### Verse 27
The waning Moon, the Sun, Mars, Rahu, Ketu, and Saturn are known as malefic <note>planets that exert a challenging or unfavorable influence</note> planets. Mercury, when in conjunction with any of these, is also considered malignant. Ketu, Mercury, and Saturn are classified as eunuchs <note>neuter in gender</note>. Venus, Rahu, and the Moon are female. The remaining planets—namely the Sun, Mars, and Jupiter—are male.

The presiding deities of the planets, starting from the Sun in their traditional order, are:
1. **The Sun:** Rudra (Shiva)
2. **The Moon:** Amba (Parvati)
3. **Mars:** Guha (Kumara/Kartikeya)
4. **Mercury:** Vishnu
5. **Jupiter:** Brahman
6. **Venus:** Lakshmi
7. **Saturn:** Kala (Yama)
8. **Rahu:** The serpent Adisesha
9. **Ketu:** Brahman <note>The creator god</note>

Fire, water, fire, earth, ether <note>space/Akasha</note>, water, and air are the elements under the control of the Sun and the other planets respectively, taken in their traditional order.

->**Wheat, rice, sesamum, Bengal gram, horsegram, pigeon pea, green gram,**<-
->**cowgram, and black gram are the grains of the Sun, Moon, Saturn, Jupiter, Ketu, Mars, Mercury, Venus, and Rahu.**<-
->**The regions are Ambura, Kalinga, Saurashtra, Avanti, Sindhu, Magadha, Yavana, the Mountains, and Kikata.**<-

### Verse 28
The Sun's grain is wheat; the Moon's is rice; the grain belonging to Saturn is sesamum; that of Jupiter is Bengal gram; and that of Ketu is horsegram. The grain signified by Mars is Dhal <note>pigeon pea</note>. Mercury represents green gram. Dolichos lablab or cowgram <gloss>mochai</gloss> is denoted by Venus, and black gram is denoted by Rahu.

The countries or regions signified by the nine planets—Rahu, the Sun, Saturn, Mars, Jupiter, Mercury, the Moon, Ketu, and Venus—are respectively:
1. **Rahu:** Ambura
2. **The Sun:** Kalinga <note>Ancient region on the east coast of India</note>
3. **Saturn:** Saurashtra <note>Modern-day Gujarat</note>
4. **Mars:** Avanti <note>Region around Ujjain</note>
5. **Jupiter:** Sindhu <note>The Indus valley region</note>
6. **Mercury:** Magadha <note>Ancient kingdom in Bihar</note>
7. **The Moon:** Yavana <note>Historically referring to the Greek or Western realms</note>
8. **Ketu:** Parvata <note>The mountainous regions</note>
9. **Venus:** Kikata <note>Ancient name for the Magadha/Bihar region</note>

### Verse 29
The Sun's gemstone is the Ruby <gloss>Manikya</gloss>. For the Moon, it is the pure, spotless Pearl <gloss>Muktaphalum</gloss>. For Mars, it is Coral <gloss>Vidruma</gloss>. For Mercury, the son of the Moon, it is the Emerald <gloss>Marakata</gloss>. For Jupiter, the teacher of the Gods, it is the Topaz <gloss>Pushparaga</gloss>. For Venus, the minister of the Asuras, it is the Diamond <gloss>Vajra</gloss>. For Saturn, it is the stainless Blue Sapphire <gloss>Nilam</gloss>. For the remaining two, Rahu and Ketu, the Agate <gloss>Gomedha</gloss> and the Lapis Lazuli or Cat's Eye <gloss>Vaidurya</gloss> are prescribed.

<keywords>planetary deities, planetary gender, five elements, sacred grains, ancient Indian geography, Navaratna, gemstones, Phaladeepika</keywords>

<page-num>21</page-num>
# Chapter Two, Verses 29-32

*Verse 29.* The ruby is the Sun’s precious stone; the pure spotless pearl which is fit to be beautifully worn belongs to the Moon. Coral belongs to Mars. Mercury’s precious stone is the emerald shaped like the bird Garuda <note>original: "Garu la"; Garuda is the mythical solar eagle and mount of Vishnu</note>. Topaz belongs to Jupiter. Venus owns diamond. Saturn’s gem is the stainless sapphire. Agate and the lapis lazuli or turquoise are said to belong to the remaining planets Rahu and Ketu <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon</note> respectively.

->Copper, bell-metal, copper ore, lead, gold, silver, and iron belong to the Sun and the other planets in order.<-
->Clothing is of their respective colors; specifically, Saturn's is ragged and Mars's is singed by fire. || 30 ||<-

*Verse 30.* The substances <term>Dhatu: minerals or metals</term> ascribed to the planets from the Sun onwards are copper, bell-metal, copper ore, lead, gold, silver and iron. The articles of apparel for the several planets will be such cloths as bear their respective colours. But the one belonging to Saturn is a rag while the one appropriate to Mars will be a cloth singed by fire.

->Pungent is the Sun's; bitter is the son of Earth's (Mars); salty is the Moon's; and Mercury's<-
->is mixed; sweet is Jupiter's; sour is Venus's; and Saturn's is astringent. || 31 ||<-

*Verse 31.* Pungent, bitter, saltish, mixed, sweet, sour (acid) and astringent are the flavours respectively of the Sun, Mars, the Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus and Saturn.

->The Sun, Jupiter, Mercury, and Mars have their marks on the right side;<-
->the others on the opposite. From the Sun, the positions are the flank, head, back,<-
->armpit, shoulder, face, and the thigh or feet. The ages from the Sun are<-
->[represented by the mnemonic] Nemi, Natha, Tata, Nakha, Naga, Shani, Jnanabdha, Nashchatana. || 32 ||<-

*Verse 32.* The Sun, Jupiter, Mercury and Mars have their characteristic mark or token on the right side. The rest have their marks on the other side. The planets <note>The text concludes by assigning specific body parts to the planets for identifying birthmarks or signs: the Sun rules the flank, the Moon the head, Mars the back, Mercury the armpit, Jupiter the shoulder, Venus the face, and Saturn the thighs or feet.</note>

# Phaladeepika Chapter II

The planets starting from the Sun onwards have marks on the hip, on the head, on the back, on the armpit, on the shoulder, on the face, and on the leg. <note>These marks or moles are used in traditional physiognomy to identify planetary influences on a person's body.</note> The Sun is a person of 50 years; the Moon is one of 70 years; Mars is 16 years old; Mercury is a lad of 20 years; Jupiter is a person 30 years old; Venus is a child of 7 years; Saturn and Rahu have each 100 years.

->Blue-black in luster, tall of body, of low caste, sinful, and of heretical belief, accompanied by hiccups;<-
->A speaker of untruth, cunning is Rahu; afflicted with skin disease, he reviles others and is devoid of intellect. || 33 ||<-
<note>original: "नीलद्युतिर्दीर्घतनुः कुवर्णः पापी सपाषण्डमतः सहिक्कः । असत्यवादी कपटी च राहुः कुष्ठी परान्निन्दति बुद्धिहीनः ॥ ३३ ॥"</note>

*Verse 33* Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, depicted as a shadow planet</note> is black in color, tall in stature, and of a low caste; he has got skin disease, he is a heretic <note>one who holds beliefs contrary to established religious doctrine</note>, he has got hiccups, he speaks falsehood, he is cunning, he suffers from leprosy, he reviles others and is devoid of intellect.

->Red and fierce of sight, with a body like a snake's front, armed, and fallen is Ketu;<-
->Smoky-hued, a smoker of smoke always, with limbs marked by wounds, lean and cruel. || 34 ||<-
<note>original: "रक्तोग्रदृष्टिर्विपगाग्रदेहः सशस्त्रः पतितश्च केतुः । धूम्रद्युतिर्धूमप एव नित्यं व्रणाङ्किताङ्गश्च कृशो नृशंसः ॥ ३४ ॥"</note>

*Verse 34* Ketu <note>The South Node of the Moon, often depicted as a headless body or a comet</note> has a red and fierce look, a venomous tongue <note>literally "a body like the front of a snake"</note>, and an elevated body; he is armed, he is an outcast, of a smoky color, and is inhaling smoke always; he has bruised limbs, he is lean and is malicious.

->Lead and an old garment belong to Rahu, while for Ketu<-
->An earthen vessel and a three-colored variegated cloth are assigned;<-
->Mercury, Saturn, and Venus are friends to both these shadow planets;<-
->Mars is said to be neutral, and the rest are enemies. || 35 ||<-
<note>original: "मीसं च जीर्णवसनं तममस्तु केतो मृद्भाजनं त्रिविधचित्रपटं प्रदिष्टम् । मित्राणि विच्छुनिमितास्तममोर्द्वयोस्तु भौमः समो निगदिता रिपवश्च शेषाः ॥ ३५ ॥"</note>

*Verse 35* Lead and an old garment belong to Rahu, while a mud vessel and a cloth of variegated color have been allotted for Ketu. Mercury, Saturn, and Venus are the friends of Rahu as well as Ketu. Mars is neutral to them. The rest <note>the Sun, Moon, and Jupiter</note> are enemies.

<summary>This page details the physical descriptions, symbolic ages, and planetary friendships of the shadow planets Rahu and Ketu, as well as the ages associated with the seven primary planets.</summary>
<keywords>Rahu, Ketu, planetary ages, Phaladeepika, Jyotisha, Vedic astrology, planetary friendships, shadow planets</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the systematic classification of planetary influences from the previous section, concluding the second chapter of the Phaladeepika.</meta>

# Chapter Two

### Verses 36-37

<note>original: "Sloka 36"</note>
Planets are considered to be in a "bad state" <note>Sanskrit: dustha</note> when they are eclipsed by the Sun's rays, positioned in their sign of debilitation <note>the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is weakest</note>, located in the house of an enemy planet, or placed in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses of the birth chart. In any other positions, they are considered to be in a "good state" <note>Sanskrit: sustha</note>. If the Moon is located in the 12th, 11th, 1st, 6th, 5th, or 7th house at the moment a question is asked regarding a large-scale irrigation project, one can confidently predict an abundance of water.

<note>original: "Sloka 37"</note>
The Sun governs trees that are tall and possess strong internal timber. The Moon and Venus are associated with creeping plants and vines. Ketu and Rahu <note>the lunar nodes, often called the shadow planets</note> produce clusters or thickets of shrubs. Mars and Saturn are responsible for the growth of thorny trees. Jupiter produces trees that bear fruit, while Mercury produces those that are barren. Venus and the Moon also govern trees that are full of sap and those that produce flowers. The Moon specifically rules over medicinal herbs; all weak or useless trees are governed by Saturn. Rahu is said to generate the Sal tree <note>Shorea robusta, a sacred and valuable timber tree in India</note>.

->Thus ends the second chapter, titled "Planets and Their Varieties," in the work *Phaladeepika* composed by the sage Mantreswara.<-

<summary>This page concludes the second chapter of the Phaladeepika by defining the auspicious and inauspicious placements of planets and categorizing the types of vegetation governed by each celestial body.</summary>
<keywords>Phaladeepika, Mantreswara, planetary dignity, debilitation, irrigation, botany, Sal tree, Rahu, Ketu, astrology and nature</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological divisions from the previous chapter, moving into the specific definitions of the Varga (divisional) systems used to determine planetary strength.</meta>

<page-num>24</page-num>
<header>Phaladeepika Chapter III</header>

## ## Chapter III

The sign, the decanate, the ninth-part, the tenth-part, the half-sign, the thirtieth-part, the seventh-part, the sixtieth-part, and the sixteenth-part—these, along with the twelfth-part, are known as the Ten Divisions. A "Vargottama" is the specific ninth-part within a sign that shares the same name as the sign itself. <note>original: "क्षेत्रत्रिभागनवभागदशांशहोरात्रिशांशसप्तमषष्टिलवाः कलांशाः। ते द्वादशांशसहिता दशवर्गसंज्ञा वर्गोत्तमो निजनिजे भवने नवांशः॥"</note>

---

### Chapter III.

*Verse 1* <term>Kshetra</term> (The full zodiacal sign or an arc of 30°), <term>Drekkana</term> (The Decanate or an arc of 10°), <term>Navamsa</term> (The ninth-part or arc of 3⅓°), <term>Dasamsa</term> (The tenth-part or an arc of 3°), <term>Hora</term> (The half-sign or an arc of 15°), <term>Trimsamsa</term> (The thirtieth-part or an arc of 1°), <term>Saptamsa</term> (The seventh-part or an arc of 4 2/7°), <term>Shashtyamsa</term> (The sixtieth-part or an arc of ½°) and <term>Kalamsa</term> (The sixteenth-part or an arc of 1⅞°)—these along with the <term>Dwadasamsa</term> (The twelfth-part or an arc of 2½°) are termed **Dasa Vargas** or the ten-fold divisions of the Zodiac. <term>Vargottama</term> (The best division) is the name given to that particular Navamsa in a Rasi <note>A Rasi is a complete zodiac sign of 30 degrees.</note> which bears the same name as that of the Rasi itself. <note>For example, if a planet is in the Aries Navamsa within the sign of Aries, it is Vargottama and considered very strong.</note>

---

If the Tenth-part, Sixtieth-part, and Sixteenth-part are omitted, the remaining seven are called the Seven Divisions. If the Seventh-part is also excluded, the remaining six are the Six Divisions. Some authorities claim the influence of the Ninth-part is equal to that of the entire Sign. <note>original: "दशांशषष्ट्यंशकलांशहीनास्ते सप्तवर्गाश्च त्रिसप्तमांशाः। षड्वर्गसंज्ञास्त्वथ राशिभावतुल्यं नवांशस्य फलं हि केचित् ॥२॥"</note>

*Verse 2.* From the aforesaid ten divisions if we omit the <term>Dasamsa</term>, <term>Shashtyamsa</term> and <term>Kalamsa</term>, the remaining 7 kinds of divisions are known as **Sapta Vargas** (Seven Divisions). If from this we exclude the <term>Saptamsa</term>, the remaining 6 kinds of divisions are known as **Shadvargas** (Six Divisions). Some are of opinion that the effect emanating from a <term>Navamsa</term> (Ninth-part) is similar (or equal) to that of the <term>Rasi</term> (The full sign).

---

The fruit of a planet is said to be full in its own sign, half in the other divisions, and a quarter in the sixteenth, tenth, and sixtieth parts. The states of a planet—Infant, Youth, Adolescent, Old, and Dead—occur in six-degree increments in even signs, and in reverse order in odd signs. <note>original: "क्षेत्रेषु पूर्णमुदितं फलमन्यवर्गेष्वद्धं कलादशमषष्टिलवेषु पादम्। बालः कुमारतरुणौ प्रवया मृतः षड्भागः क्रमाद्युजि विपर्ययमित्यवस्थाः"</note>

<summary>This page defines the mathematical subdivisions of the zodiac signs (Vargas), categorizing them into groups of six, seven, and ten, and introduces the concept of Vargottama and planetary age states.</summary>
<keywords>Varga, Navamsa, Vargottama, Shadvarga, Saptavarga, Dasavarga, planetary strength, zodiac divisions</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical definitions of planetary strengths and divisional charts (vargas) from the previous chapter, focusing on the specific mathematical portions of a zodiac sign.</meta>

<page-num>25</page-num>
<header>Verses 3-4 Chapter Three 25</header>

---

*Verse 3.* The effect <note>The strength or influence of a planet</note> is full in the Signs <note>original: "Rasis"</note>. In the other divisional charts <note>original: "vargas"</note> it is half; and in the sixteenth, tenth, and sixtieth divisions <note>original: "Shodasamsa, Dasamsa and Shashtyamsa"</note>, it is one-fourth. In an odd-numbered Sign, planets are said to be in infancy, in boyhood, in adolescence, in advanced age, and *at the point of death* <note>original: "in extremis"</note> according to the first, second, third, fourth, or the last six-degree portions respectively advanced by them in the sign. But in an even sign, these several divisions have to be made regularly in the reverse order.

->Half of a sign is indeed a *Hora*; in an odd sign, these belong to the Sun and the Moon <note>original: "Ravi and Sudhanshu"</note>; in an even sign, this is reversed. The lords of the *Drekkanas* <note>one-third of a sign, or 10 degrees</note> are the lords of the sign itself, the 5th house (the house of the son), and the 9th house (the house of fortune). The owners of the *Dwadasamsas* <note>one-twelfth portions, or 2.5 degrees</note> are to be counted starting from that sign itself. In an odd sign, the *Trimsamsas* <note>one-thirtieth portions or degrees</note> allotted to Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus are respectively 5, 5, 8, 7, and 5 degrees. In an even sign, it is reversed. That is, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and Mars have 5, 7, 8, 5, and 5 degrees respectively. The first *Navamsa* <note>one-ninth portion</note> in the several signs starting from Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> onwards commence respectively with Aries, Capricorn, Libra, and Cancer.<-

*Verse 4.* *Hora* means half of a Sign; in an odd sign, the halves belong respectively to the Sun and the Moon, and in an even one, to the Moon and the Sun. The Drekkana or the third portions of a sign, are owned by the lords of the sign itself, of the son's or the 5th house and of the 9th or the house of Good Fortune <note>original: "Subha"</note>. The owners of the *Dwadasamsas* or 1/12th portions of a sign are to be reckoned from that sign. In an odd sign, the *Trimsamsas* or degrees, *i. e.*, 1/30th portions allotted to Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury and Venus are respectively 5, 5, 8, 7 and 5. In an even sign, it is reversed. That is, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn and Mars have 5, 7, 8, 5 and 5 degrees respectively. The first *Navamsa* in the several signs from Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> onwards commence respectively with Aries, Capricorn <note>original: "Makara"</note>, Libra <note>original: "Tula"</note> and Cancer <note>original: "Kataka"</note>.

<note>The following mnemonic line uses the Katapayadi system, a Sanskrit numerical notation, to encode astronomical or mathematical data.</note>
Sacrifice, gem, person, wealth, lead, cloth, form, parrot, by the maid-servant, elephant, union, bird, strength, fortune, stone, dust, no fuel, dig deep.

# Phaladeepika Chapter III

<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>Phaladeepika Chapter III</header>

---

->The 60th divisions (Shashtyamsas) in an odd sign are designated as "Cruel" in the following order: 1, 2, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 15, 16, 30, 31, 32, 33, 34, 35, 39, 40, 42, 43, 44, 48, 51, 52, and 59.<-
->The remaining are called "Auspicious." In even signs, this is reversed. ॥ 5 ॥<-

*Verse 5.* In the case of an odd sign <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc.</note> the <term>Krura Shashtyamsas</term> <note>original: "Krura" meaning cruel or harsh</note> or unpropitious 1/60th portions are 1st, 2nd, 8th, 9th, 10th, 11th, 12th, 15th, 16th, 30th, 31st, 32nd, 33rd, 34th, 35th, 39th, 40th, 42nd, 43rd, 44th, 48th, 51st, 52nd and 59th. The rest are <term>Saumya</term> <note>original: "Saumya" meaning gentle or lunar</note> or propitious ones. In the case of even signs <note>Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, etc.</note>, it is reverse, that is—the <term>Shashty-amsa</term> portions stated as <term>Krura</term> in the odd signs are the propitious or <term>Saumya</term> ones in the even signs and *vice versa*.

->The 7th and 10th divisions begin from the sign itself in odd signs; in even signs, they begin from the 7th and 9th respectively.<-
->The lords of the 16th divisions are the deities Brahma, Vishnu, Shiva, and the Sun; in even signs, this is reversed.<-
->The strength of these divisions is known by their association with trines, own signs, exaltations, and quadrants;<-
->others state these divisions number 7, 10, or 13. ॥ 6 ॥<-

*Verse 6.* The <term>Sapthamsas</term> <note>The 1/7th division of a zodiac sign</note> or the 1/7th portions are in the case of an odd sign to be counted regularly from the sign itself, while in the case of an even sign they are to be reckoned from the 7th sign onwards. In the case of an odd sign, the <term>Dasamamsas</term> <note>The 1/10th division of a zodiac sign</note> or 1/10th portions are to be counted from the sign itself; in the case of an even sign, from its 9th sign onwards. The lords of the <term>Shodasamsas</term> <note>The 1/16th division of a zodiac sign</note> or the 1/16th sub-divisions in the case of an odd sign are those of the 12 signs reckoned from that sign onward and the deities <term>Brahma</term> <note>The Creator</note>, <term>Vishnu</term> <note>The Preserver</note>, <term>Hara</term> <note>Shiva, the Destroyer</note> and <term>Ravi</term> <note>The Sun</note>. In the case of an even sign, this is reversed. The renowned or auspicious parts of great excellence arise from the several <term>Vargas</term> <note>Sub-divisional charts</note> (<term>Rasi</term> <note>Sign</note>, <term>Hora</term> <note>1/2 division</note>, <term>Drekkana</term> <note>1/3 division</note>, etc.,) by the combination of the special characteristics of <term>Trikona</term> <note>Trine</note>, <term>Moolatrikonabhavana</term> <note>Root-trine position</note>, <term>Swakshetra</term> <note>Own sign</note>, <term>Swochcha</term> <note>Exaltation</note>, <term>Kendra</term> <note>Quadrant</note> and <term>Vargottama</term> <note>Occupying the same sign in the main chart and the 1/9th chart</note> and number 7 in the case of the <term>Saptha Vargas</term> <note>The 7-fold division system</note> and 10 in the case of the <term>Dasa Vargas</term> <note>The 10-fold division system</note>. Others reckon them as 13.

<summary>This page details the mathematical calculation and classification of the 60th, 7th, 10th, and 16th sub-divisions of zodiac signs, distinguishing between auspicious and inauspicious portions. It also introduces the concept of planetary strength derived from these various divisional systems.</summary>
<keywords>Shashtyamsa, Sapthamsa, Dasamamsa, Shodasamsa, Varga, Krura, Saumya, Hindu Astrology, Phaladeepika, planetary strength</keywords>
