->Arrange the thirteen divisions <note>original: "vargas"</note> in order, according to whether the planet is in a friendly sign, its own sign, or its exaltation sign.<-
->By the sum of these divisions—numbering two, three, five, four, nine, seven, eight, and six—<-
->The sages speak of the divisions known as Parijata, Uttama, Gopura,<-
->Simhasana, Paravata, Devaloka, Suraloka, and Iravata. ॥ 7 ॥<-

**Verse 7** Consider with reference to the 13 <term>vargas</term> <note>The thirteen divisional charts used in Vedic astrology to determine a planet's hidden strength.</note> a planet's position, whether it is in a friendly house, its own house, or its exaltation house. You will have the following kinds of <term>Vaiseshikamsas</term> <note>Special distinctions or "extraordinary portions" gained by a planet being in favorable signs across multiple divisional charts.</note> resulting: The combination of 2 vargas is called <term>Parijata</term> (Celestial Coral Tree); the union of 3 vargas is termed <term>Uttama</term> (Excellent); that of 4, <term>Gopura</term> (City Gate). When 5 vargas combine, it is called <term>Simhasana</term> (Throne). The union of 6 vargas is called <term>Paravata</term> (Pigeon/Dove). The combination of 7 vargas is called <term>Devaloka</term> (Realm of the Gods); that of 8 vargas, <term>Suraloka</term> (Realm of the Celestials). <term>Iravata</term> (The Celestial Elephant) is the name given for the union of 9 vargas.

Suppose Jupiter's position is 8—1—25—1 or the 2nd degree of Sagittarius <note>original: "Dhanus"</note>. He occupies his (1) <term>Moolatrikona</term> <note>A planet's "root" or primary favorable sign, stronger than its own sign but less than exaltation.</note> (2) <term>Drekkana</term> <note>The 1/3rd division of a sign.</note> (3) <term>Saptamsa</term> <note>The 1/7th division of a sign.</note> (4) <term>Dasamsa</term> <note>The 1/10th division of a sign.</note> (5) <term>Dwadasamsa</term> <note>The 1/12th division of a sign.</note> and (6) <term>Kalamsa</term> <note>The 1/16th division of a sign.</note>. Thus Jupiter must be declared to have atta<unclear>in</unclear>ed a Paravatamsa.

->The Parijata division makes one respectable, with moderate wealth and happiness;<-
->If situated in the Uttama division, one is well-behaved, modest, and clever.<-
->A planet in the Gopura portion grants a virtuous mind, one's own lands, and a home;<-
->One in the Simhasana makes a man a favorite of kings or equal to a king. ॥ 8 ॥<-

# Phaladeepika, Chapter III

A Simhasanamsa <note>original: "Simhasanamsa" refers to a planet occupying five favorable divisions or 'vargas'</note> will cause the native to become the friend of a king or one on a par with him.

-> He who attains the Paravata portion possesses the wealth of excellent horses and elephants; <-
-> He in the Devaloka portion becomes a ruler of the earth with great fame; <-

*Verse 9.* The planet that has attained a Paravatamsa <note>6 favorable divisions</note> will make the native endowed with very good horses, elephants, vehicles and other princely appendages. The person in whose nativity a planet has reached a Devalokamsa <note>7 favorable divisions</note> will be a king widely renowned for his good qualities. The planet that has gained an Iravatamsa <note>8 favorable divisions</note> in a person's nativity will make him a second God Indra <note>The King of the Gods in Hindu mythology</note> fit to be saluted by other kings. The planet in a Suralokamsa <note>9 favorable divisions</note> secures to the native good luck, wealth, corn, children and kingship.

-> Now I shall describe in order the effects of planets being weak in all divisions: death; <-
-> Then destruction, grief, misfortune, lack of happiness, love of kin, and excellence; <-
-> Favor of kings, wealth, royalty, and the best of kings when strong in all divisions; <-
-> Prosperity, happiness, and royalty; while sickness and death are the fruits of the infant and other states. ॥ 10 ॥ <note>original Sanskrit: "यद्वर्गेष्वखिलेषु..." This verse correlates the number of 'weak' or 'strong' divisions to specific life outcomes.</note>

The author now describes the nature of the <term>decanates</term><note>A decanate, or Drekkana, is a 10-degree division of a zodiac sign. Each 30-degree sign contains three decanates, each with its own symbolic imagery and influence.</note>—

->The last decanates of Leo, Aries, Sagittarius, Libra, and Gemini, as well as the first of Aries and Sagittarius, and the middle of Virgo and Gemini, are the weapon-bearers. The middle of Scorpio is the noose.<-
->The first of Capricorn is the fetters; the first of Leo and Aquarius and the middle of Libra are vulture-faced.<-
->The last of Taurus is a bird, and the first of Cancer has the face of a boar.<-

*Verse 13.* The last decanates of Leo, Aries, Sagittarius, Libra and Gemini, the first ones of Sagittarius and Aries, the middle ones of Virgo and Gemini are termed <term>Ayudha Drekkana</term><note>original: "आयुध द्रेक्काण"</note> or 'armed' decanates. The middle decanate of Scorpio is called <term>Pasa</term><note>original: "पाश" meaning noose</note>. The first decanate of Capricorn is called <term>Nigala</term><note>original: "निगल" meaning fetters or shackles</note> decanate. The first decanates of Leo and Aquarius and the middle one of Libra are vulture-faced. The last decanate of Taurus is a bird <note>original: "पक्षि" - Pakshi</note> and the first decanate of Cancer has a face like that of a pig.

->The first of Scorpio, the last of Cancer, and the last of Pisces are serpents. The middle of Aries and Taurus, and the first and last of Leo and Scorpio are four-footed.<-
->In these, the results are poverty, cruelty, and reproach. In dual signs, the decanates are bad, middling, and good respectively; in movable signs, it is the reverse.<-
->In fixed signs, the experts say the results of the rising decanates are bad, good, and middling.<-

*Verse 14.* The first decanate of Scorpio, the last one of Cancer and the last one of Pisces are termed serpent decanates. The second of Aries and Taurus, the first of Leo and the last of Scorpio are called quadruped <note>four-footed</note> decanates. Persons born in these (i.e., when these decanates are rising) will be without wealth, cruel, and blamed by others. In <term>dual signs</term><note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces</note>, the three decanates yield bad, moderate, and good results respectively. In <term>movable signs</term><note>Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn</note>, this order is reversed <note>good, moderate, then bad</note>. In <term>fixed signs</term><note>Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius</note>, the experts say the results are bad, good, and then moderate.

->The Moon, if placed in her own or a friend's group, bestows a beautiful form and virtue;<-

*Verse 17* The Moon, if posited in her own or a friend's decanate <note>A 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</note>, will make the native beautiful in appearance and possessed of good qualities. <margin>}</margin>->In exaltation, a planet is blazing; in its special trine, it is happy; in its own home, it is confident; in a friend's house, it is delighted.<-
<margin>}</margin>->It is calm when joined with the divisions of a gentle planet; it is considered powerful when its rays are clear and bright.<-

*Verse 18* A planet is "blazing" when he is in his exaltation; he is "happy" in his <term>Moolatrikona</term> <note>A specific favorable placement in a sign, second only to exaltation</note>; he is "confident" in his own house; he is "delighted" in a friend's house; "calm" when he has reached the <term>Varga</term> <note>Sub-divisional chart</note> of a benefic <note>A planet that naturally produces positive results, like Jupiter or Venus</note> planet. He is "capable" when he shines bright with unclouded splendour.

->Overcome by another planet, he is tortured; joined with the divisions of a cruel planet, he is base.<-
->In an enemy's house, the lord of planets is deeply miserable; in his fall, he is terrified; when set, he is crippled.<-

*Verse 19* <note>original: "Slola 19"</note> He is "tortured" when overcome by another planet <note>referring to planetary war or "Graha Yuddha"</note>. He is "base" by union with the <term>Varga</term> of a malefic <note>A planet that naturally produces harsh results, like Saturn or Mars</note>.

# || Chapter Four ||

->The strength is said to be sixfold: Temporal strength, Motional strength, Exaltation strength,<-

->Mars, the Moon, and Venus are strong at night; the others by day; Mercury is always strong. Benefics are strong in the bright fortnight,<-

### Chapter IV.

*Verse 1.* The strength of a planet is said to be sixfold and they are in the following order:—(1) <term>Kalaja</term> (<note>original: "कालज"</note> Temporal strength), (2) <term>Cheshta</term> (<note>original: "चेष्टा"</note> Motional strength), (3) <term>Ucchaja</term> (<note>original: "उच्चज"</note> Exaltation strength), (4) <term>Dig</term> (<note>original: "दिक्"</note> Directional strength), (5) <term>Ayana</term> (<note>original: "अयन"</note> Equinoctial or declination strength), or

<sig>Phala - Dee - 5</sig>

# Phaladeepika Chapter IV

...that derived from its declination (north or south) and (6) <term>Sthana</term> <note>original: "स्थान" (Sthāna)</note> or positional strength. In the night-time, Mars, the Moon, and Venus are strong; Mercury is strong at all times. The rest <note>The Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn</note> are strong during the day. In the bright half of a month <note>the waxing moon</note> the benefics are strong, and in the dark half <note>the waning moon</note>, the malefics are strong. Planets, when they happen to be the lord of the year, month, day, and hour, get 1/4, 1/2, 3/4, and 1 <term>Rupa</term> <note>a unit of planetary strength</note> respectively.

->The Moon has motional strength when full; the Sun in his northern course; the others when retrograde.<-
->In war, those in the north with great brilliance win; in exaltation, strength is highest at the peak.<-
->Directional strength: Sun and Mars in the south; Moon and Venus in the north; Jupiter and Mercury in the east;<-
->Saturn in the west. Mercury, Saturn, and Moon are strong in the southern path; others in the northern.<-

*Verse 2* The Moon gets <term>Cheshtabala</term> <note>motional strength</note> when she is full. The Sun gets it when he is in his northern course <note>the period between the winter and summer solstices</note>, the other planets when in their retrograde motion. In planetary war <note>when two planets are in close conjunction</note>, those that are posited in the north and who have got brilliant rays should be considered as victorious. Planets have their full <term>Uchcha bala</term> <note>exaltation strength</note> when in their highest exaltation. As regards directional strength <note>Dig Bala</note>, the Sun and Mars are strong in the 10th house <note>the Midheaven or South</note>, Venus and the Moon in the 4th house <note>the Nadir or North</note>, Mercury and Jupiter in the 1st house <note>the Ascendant or East</note>, and Saturn in the 7th house <note>the Descendant or West</note>. Mercury, Saturn, and the Moon have their <term>Ayana bala</term> <note>strength from declination</note> in their southern course. The rest have the same in their northern course.

->Strong in exaltation, own sign, or friend's sign; or in the six divisions of the zodiac.<-
->Positional strength is full, half, or quarter in angles, succedent, and cadent houses.<-
->Planets are strong in the middle, beginning, or end of signs; Saturn, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Moon, and Sun<-
->increase in strength according to their natural speed and distance.<-

*Verse 3* The strength accruing to a planet in the six <term>vargas</term> <note>divisional charts</note> (namely <term>Rasi</term> <note>the main sign</note>, <term>Hora</term> <note>half-sign</note>, <term>Drekkana</term> <note>one-third sign</note>, <term>Navamsa</term> <note>one-ninth sign</note>, <term>Dwadasamsa</term> <note>one-twelfth sign, continued on next page</note>...

<header>Verses 4-5 Chapter Four 35</header>

<term>Dasamsa</term> <note>a one-tenth division of a zodiac sign</note> and <term>Trimsamsa</term> <note>a one-thirtieth division of a zodiac sign</note> on a consideration of its being posited in its exaltation, its own and its friendly signs etc., is termed its positional strength. That arising from its being in <term>Kendra</term> <note>angular houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</note> and other <term>bhavas</term> <note>astrological houses</note> (i.e., <term>Phanapara</term> <note>succedent houses: 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th</note> and <term>Apoklima</term> <note>cadent houses: 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th</note>) will be 1, 1/2 and 1/4 <term>Rupa</term> <note>a unit of measure for planetary strength</note> respectively. The hermaphrodite planets <note>Mercury and Saturn</note> are strong in the middle, the male ones at the initial and the female ones at the concluding portions of a <term>Rasi</term> <note>a zodiac sign</note>. Saturn, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, the Moon and the Sun are in their order increasing in their natural strength.

---

->Even if a planet is in its sign of depression or an enemy's sign,<-
->it is considered strong if it is in retrograde motion<-
->or if it is full of brilliant rays.<-
->Conversely, like the Moon, a planet in its exaltation or own sign<-
->becomes weak if its rays are eclipsed or lost.<-

*Verse 4.* <note>original: "Sloka 4"</note> A planet is said to possess strength when he is retrograde or when his rays are full and brilliant, though posited in a depression or inimical sign or division. Like the Moon, a planet though occupying an exaltation, friendly or his own sign or division becomes weak should his rays be overpowered or eclipsed <note>this refers to combustion, where a planet is too close to the Sun</note>.

->All planets are strong in exaltation; the Moon's strength is in her phase,<-
->The Sun's strength is in his direction; the star-planets are strong in retrograde.<-
->Rahu is strong in Cancer, Taurus, Aries, Aquarius, Scorpio, and Gemini;<-
->Ketu is strong in the latter half of Virgo, Leo, Sagittarius, and the Sun-Moon conjunction at night.<-

*Verse 5.* <note>original: "Sloka 5"</note> All planets are strong when they are in exaltation. The Moon is strong and auspicious when she has her full <term>Paksha bala</term> <note>strength derived from the lunar phase; the waxing Moon</note> and the Sun when he has his full <term>Dig-bala</term> <note>directional strength; specifically when the Sun is at the highest point in the sky</note> (i.e., when he is in the 10th house or the Meridian). The other five non-luminous planets <note>Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn</note> are strong when they are retrograde. Rahu in Cancer, Taurus...

<note>original: "shabhı"</note> <insert>Vri</insert>shabha <note>Taurus</note>, Mesha <note>Aries</note>, Kumbha <note>Aquarius</note> and Vrischika <note>Scorpio</note>; and Ketu <note>the South Node of the Moon</note> in Meena <note>Pisces</note>, Kanya <note>Virgo</note>, Vrishabha <note>Taurus</note> and the later half of Dhanus <note>Sagittarius</note>, in Parivesha <note>an atmospheric halo</note> and Indra chapa <note>a rainbow</note> are strong if there be a conjunction of the Moon and the Sun and the time be night.

---

->**The strength of the first house is one unit in human signs, one-fourth in Scorpio, and one-half in others.**<-
->**It is equal to the strength of its lord, and extremely powerful when the lord is in an increasing house.**<-
->**When joined or aspected by its lord, Jupiter, or Mercury, or joined by Venus and not others,**<-
->**Night-signs are powerful at night, and others are said to be powerful during the day. || 6 ||**<-

*Verse 6.* If the first <term>bhava</term> <note>astrological house</note> be a biped <note>human</note> sign, one <term>rupa</term> <note>a unit of measure for planetary strength</note> has to be allotted to it; if it be <term>Vrischika</term> <note>Scorpio</note>, the <term>bala</term> <note>strength</note> is 1/4; if it be any other sign, the strength is 1/2. The strength of the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or 1st house</note> is equal to that of its lord and is very powerful when the lord occupies an <term>Upachaya</term> <note>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses, associated with growth</note> house. The same is also the case when it is occupied or aspected by its lord, Jupiter, or Mercury, or when it is associated with Venus and is at the same time without the conjunction or aspect of any other planet. The day-signs possess strength in day-births while night-signs are declared powerful in night-births.

---

->**Full strength in exaltation, three-quarters in the root-trine, half in one's own sign, and a quarter in a friend's sign.**<-
->**In an enemy's sign it is very little; in debilitation or when eclipsed, the strength of the sign is fruitless for men. || 7 ||**<-

*Verse 7.* <note>original: "Slola 7"</note> A planet gets one full <term>rupa</term> <note>unit of strength</note> as positional strength when he is in his exaltation <term>Rasi</term> <note>zodiac sign</note>. The strength is 3/4ths when posited in his <term>Moolatrikona Rasi</term> <note>a specific favorable position, the "root-trine"</note>. It is 1/2 when the planet occupies his own <term>Rasi</term>. When the planet is in a friendly house, his <term>bala</term> <note>strength</note> is only 1/4. In an inimical house, he gets only very little. The strength is nil when he is in his depression <note>debilitation</note> sign or eclipsed by the Sun's rays.

### The strength of planets in the angular houses <note>original: "Kendras"</note> is said to be found in the fourth, the tenth, the seventh, and the rising sign <note>original: "Lagna"</note>.
### In the order of the positions mentioned above, they consider the increase of strength by degrees. || 8 ||

*Verse 8* The strength of planets when occupying the four angular houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th) has been distinguished by the astrologers in the following manner: It is one-quarter in the 4th house, one-half in the 10th, three-quarters in the 7th, and full in the 1st or rising sign, thus increasing by one-quarter in each stage.

### The aspect of the seventh house alone is to be called the best in all cases; other aspects are not so.
### In planetary combinations <note>original: "Yogas"</note> and the like, some say the special aspects <note>referring to the unique sight of Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note> give lesser results, but this is not stated by all authorities. || 9 ||

*Verse 9* The aspect from the 7th house is the only one that should be declared as most effective in all cases, and not the others. But there are others who opine that the special aspect (of Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars) is in no way less efficacious in producing full effect in all planetary combinations and the like.

### Natural enmity or friendship alone should be the standard for producing results properly.
### Temporal <note>temporary or situational</note> friendship and enmity should be declared according to the specific circumstances; such relationships are not permanent. || 10 ||

*Verse 10* Natural enmity or friendship between two planets is more fully effective than and preferable to their corresponding temporal ones, as the latter is only a variable quantity and not a permanent one.

### In removing all evils and in increasing prosperity,
### The power of Jupiter is greater than all other planets.
### Mercury and Venus possess a quarter and a half of that power respectively,
### While the strength of the Moon is the seed of the power of all planets. || 11 ||

*Verse 11* In completely warding off evil and in promoting prosperity, Jupiter is the most powerful of all the planets. Mercury and Venus have a quarter and a half <insert>of Jupiter's strength</insert>...

# In the Phaladeepika, Chapter IV

The Moon's strength serves as a basis, as it were, for the strength of all the planets.

## Regarding the Moon’s Actions and States

Divide the elapsed portion of the birth star <note>the specific lunar mansion or Nakshatra occupied at birth</note> reduced to vighatikas <note>a unit of time equal to 24 seconds</note> by the numbers 60, 300, and 100.

The three resulting quotients are termed (1) <term>Chandra Kriya</term> <note>Lunar Action</note>, (2) <term>Chandra Avastha</term> <note>Lunar State</note>, and (3) <term>Chandra Vela</term> <note>Lunar Time/Sub-division</note> respectively. Their effects are described below.

<margin>||</margin> Verse 12. Find what portion of the asterism occupied by the Moon has elapsed at birth. Reduce the same to vighatikas. Divide this result by (1) 60, (2) 300, and (3) 100. The three resulting quotients are termed (1) <term>Chandra Kriya</term> (2) <term>Chandra Avastha</term> and (3) <term>Chandra Vela</term> respectively. Their effects are described below.

## The Results of the Moon’s Actions (Chandra Kriya)

# Chapter Four: Verses 17-20
<note>original: चतुर्थोऽध्याय:</note>

*Verse 17* The effects of the Moon's 36 <term>Velas</term> <note>Vela (वेला) refers to specific time-divisions or "tides" of lunar influence used to determine the quality of a particular moment.</note> have been thus described by the wise:—(1) headache (2) pleasure (3) performing a sacrifice (4) living happily (5) eye disease (6) being happy (7) amusement with young damsels (8) violent fever (9) adornment with golden jewels (10) shedding tears (11) devouring poison (12) coition (13) stomach ache

*Verse 20* The <term>Chandra kriya</term> <note>Literally "Lunar Action," referring to the 60 specific activities the Moon is said to be engaged in at any given moment.</note> and others described above will be found to be useful and productive of effect in the case of a <term>Jataka</term> <note>A natal birth chart.</note>, a <term>muhurta</term> <note>The selection of an auspicious time for starting an event.</note> and a query <note>Prashna, or horary astrology, where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked.</note>. One ought therefore to particularly examine them and then only set about predicting.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary strengths from Chapter IV of the Phaladeepika, focusing on the specific numerical requirements for a planet to be considered powerful.</meta>

<page-num>42</page-num>
->On the Phaladeepika, Chapter IV<-

The strength arising from the lunar phase <note>original: "Pakshabala"</note> is said to be distinctive for the Moon, while the strength arising from position <note>original: "Sthanabala"</note> is more significant for the other planets. When this is combined with the other types of strength, the planets become increasingly powerful. There are many other similar types of strength belonging to them.

*Verse 21.* <term>Pakshabala</term> <note>Strength derived from the waxing or waning phase of the moon</note> is special to the Moon, while the <term>strength</term> <note>original: "bala"</note> arising from <term>Position</term> <note>original: "Stthana"</note> is important for the other planets. This, when added to the other kinds of strength of the several planets, is capable of outdoing one another. Other kinds of strength, there are many, similar to this.

### || Total Numerical Strength ||

The Sun is strong with six and a half units; the Moon with six; the son of the Earth <note>Mars</note> with five. The son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note> is like the Sun, while for the Teacher <note>Jupiter</note>, it is six and a half; then Venus is strong with five and a half.

*Verse 22.* The Sun is declared strong when his strength is 6½ <term>rupas</term> <note>A 'rupa' is a standard unit of measure in Vedic astrology used to quantify planetary strength</note>. In the case of the Moon, it is 6 <term>rupas</term>. Five <term>rupas</term> are assigned to Mars and 7 to Mercury. Jupiter's <term>Full Strength</term> <note>original: "Purnabala"</note> is similar to that of the Sun, that is, 6½ <term>rupas</term>. Venus is strong when he gets 5½ <term>rupas</term>.

Saturn is indeed strong with five; this is the combination of the six-fold strengths, otherwise they are weak. Thus, having well considered the strengths and weaknesses of the planets, one should predict the results.

*Verse 23.* Saturn should have 5 <term>rupas</term>. These are the figures representing the total <term>Six-fold Strengths</term> <note>original: "Shadbalas"; a mathematical system used to determine the potency of a planet based on six different criteria</note> for the several planets. If the respective figures be otherwise, *i.e.,* less than the above, the planets should be considered as weak. It is only after a minute examination of the strength or otherwise of the <term>Shadbalas</term> of the several planets, one ought to declare their effects.

When one unit is added to the total strength of the lord of the Ascendant and other houses, that is called the total strength of that house... in whichever direction the directional strength lies, that constitutes the strength of the house, combined with the planetary aspects.

*Verse 24.* <note>This verse introduces the calculation for the strength of the astrological houses or 'Bhavas' based on their ruling planets and directional influences.</note>

<summary>This page details the minimum numerical values (rupas) required for each planet to be considered strong within the Shadbala system and emphasizes the importance of these calculations before making astrological predictions.</summary>

<page-num>43</page-num>
<header>Verse 1 Chapter Five 43</header>

->॥ Thus ends the fourth chapter, titled "The Determination of the Six-fold Strengths," in the Phaladeepika composed by Mantreswara. ॥<-

*Verse 24* In the case of the Ascendant (<term>Lagna</term> <note>The point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</note>) and other houses (<term>bhavas</term>), add one unit (<term>rupa</term>) to the strength of the lord of the house concerned. Supplement this by the directional strength (<term>Digbala</term> <note>original: "दिग्बल"</note>) due to that house and also by the strength of aspect (<term>Drigbala</term> <note>original: "दृग्बल"; the influence of other planets looking upon the house</note>) of that house. The aggregate sum total is the house strength (<term>Bhava bala</term> <note>original: "भावबल"</note>) required.

For more details about the several kinds of strength of planets, please see Chapters II and III of my edition of the *Sripati-paddhati* <note>A classic Sanskrit treatise on mathematical astrology</note>.

Thus ends the 4th Chapter (<term>Adhyaya</term>) on "Determination of the Six-fold Strengths (<term>Shadbalas</term>) of Planets" in the work *Phaladeepika* <note>A major 16th-century text on predictive astrology</note> composed by Mantreswara.

->from the Ascendant or the Moon; whether from the father, mother, enemy, friend, brother, or wife;<-
->or from a servant, depending on which among the Sun, the Ascendant, or the Moon is strongest.<-
->Experts say the profession is determined by the lord of the Navamsa sign occupied by the lord of the 10th. ॥ 1 ॥<-

### Chapter V.

*Verse 1.* One ought to declare the acquisition of wealth by any person with reference to the predominance of the Ascendant (<term>Lagna</term>) or the Moon as the case may be in his horoscope. The source of such an acquisition will be the father, mother, a foe, a friend, a brother, a wife or a servant, according as the Sun or any of the other planets taken in order occupies the 10th place from the Ascendant or the Moon whichever is stronger. His profession will be that prescribed for the lord of the <term>Navamsa</term> <note>A sub-divisional chart representing one-ninth of a zodiac sign, used to determine the hidden strength and specific fruits of a planet's position</note>...

->If the lord of the Navamsa <note>The 1/9th divisional chart used to determine the hidden strength of planets</note> is strong, wealth is obtained without effort; if weak, the results are meager. One should predict the acquisition of wealth in the region signified by the 10th house or its Navamsa. <margin>
[
</margin>

*Verse 9.* When the planet owning the Navamsa is strong, there will be acquisition of wealth without any exertion. If it should be weak, very little wealth accrues. The country of acquisition will be that signified by the sign representing the 10th house or the Navamsa occupied by the lord of the 10th house. But if this sign be occupied or aspected by its lord, he will earn in his own country. The same will be the result if the Navamsa occupied by the lord of the 10th house be an immovable Rasi <note>original: "Sthira Rasi" - signs like Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, or Aquarius that represent stability</note>. And if the said sign be occupied or aspected by planets other than its lord, the native will earn in a place other than his own native country.

Thus ends the 5th Chapter on "Profession and Livelihood" in the work *Phaladeepika* composed by Mantreswara.

# Chapter Six

<page-num>47</page-num>
->Chapter Six<-
Slokas 2-3

...are formed by the planets—Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn respectively when each of them occupies a <term>Kendra</term> <note>A "Kendra" refers to the four cardinal houses of a horoscope (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th), which are considered the pillars of a person's life.</note> identical with its <term>Own Sign</term> <note>original: "Swakshetra"</note> or <term>Exaltation</term> <note>original: "Swochcha"</note>.

> The one born in Ruchaka has a long face, acquires wealth through many daring deeds, is a hero, a slayer of enemies, and powerful.
> He is very proud, famous for his virtues, a commander of armies, and a conqueror.
> Long-lived, with a skillful and pure intellect, praised by the learned,
> A king is born of Bhadra yoga, possessing great wealth and creating a stir in the assembly. || 2 ||

Verse 2. The person born in a <term>Ruchaka</term> yoga <note>A yoga formed by a strong Mars.</note> will have a broad face, will acquire wealth by doing many daring deeds, will be brave, will overcome his enemies, will be powerful, and will be arrogant. He will become renowned for his merits, will be a leader of an army, and will emerge victorious in all his attempts. The man born in a <term>Bhadra</term> yoga <note>A yoga formed by a strong Mercury.</note> will be gifted with long life and keen intellect, will be clean, praised by the learned, will lead men, will be very rich, and clever in addressing an assembly.

> In Hamsa, one is a king praised by the virtuous, with marks of the conch, lotus, fish, and elephant-goad
> On the feet and hands; possessing a beautiful body, eating pure food, and being righteous.
> With well-developed limbs, resolute, wealthy, blessed with good children, royalty, and fortune;
> In Malavya, one enjoys pleasures, possesses good vehicles and fame, is learned, and has calm senses. || 3 ||

Verse 3. The person born in the <term>Hamsa</term> yoga <note>A yoga formed by a strong Jupiter.</note> will be a king extolled by the good. He will have in his legs and hands marks of <term>Conch</term> <note>original: "Sankha"</note>, <term>Lotus</term> <note>original: "Padma"</note>, <term>Fish</term> <note>original: "Matsya"</note>, and <term>Elephant-goad</term> <note>original: "Ankusa"</note> <term>Lines</term> <note>original: "Rekhas"; these are auspicious symbols in palmistry and podoscopy.</note>. He will possess a beautiful body, will eat pure food, and will be of a righteous disposition. The man born in a <term>Malavya</term> yoga <note>A yoga formed by a strong Venus.</note> will have strong limbs, will be resolute, wealthy, endowed with wife, children, and happiness, will be prosperous—

->Praised by all, possessing good servants and strength, a village headman or a king,<-
->of wicked disposition, the one born in Sasa Yoga pursues the wives and wealth of others, yet remains happy.<-
->These five yogas, when reckoned from either the Ascendant or the Moon, grant imperial power;<-
->with one, two, or more of these, one becomes fortunate, king-like, a king, an emperor, or even greater.<-

*Verse 4.* The person born in the <term>Sasa yoga</term> <note>A "Rabbit" combination formed when Saturn is in its own or exaltation sign in a quadrant</note> will be extolled by all, will have good servants, will be strong, will be the headman of a village or a king, will be wicked in disposition, will have intrigues with women not his own, will usurp others' wealth and be happy. Just as these five yogas arise when reckoned from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note>, similarly they are possible when counted even from the Moon's place. The person who happens to have one, two, three, four or all the five yogas will respectively be a (1) fortunate man (2) one equal to a king (3) a king, (4) an emperor, and (5) one superior to No 4.

->When planets other than the Sun occupy the second, the twelfth, or both<-
->positions from the Moon, Sunapha, Anapha, and Durudhara are formed; otherwise, it is Kemadruma.<-
->If planets are in a quadrant from the Moon, or if planets occupy the quadrants<-
->from the Ascendant, some say Kemadruma does not exist.<-

*Verse 5.* When planets other than the Sun occupy the 2nd, the 12th or both houses reckoned from the Moon, the resulting 3 yogas are respectively styled <term>Sunapha</term>, <term>Anapha</term> and <term>Durudhara</term>. In the absence of the 3 yogas defined above, there is the <term>Kemadruma</term> yoga <note>A combination often associated with poverty or loneliness unless cancelled by other factors</note>. There are some who declare that there is no <term>Kemadruma</term> where a <term>Kendra</term> <note>A quadrant or angular house: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note> or the

*Verse 6*   The person who has had his birth in the **Sunapha** <note>original: "सुनफा" (Sunaphā)</note> <term>yoga</term> <note>A "yoga" is a specific planetary combination or "union" that produces a particular result in a person's life.</note> will be a king or his equal, with self-acquired property, and renowned for his wisdom and wealth. *Verse 7.*   Taking freely to the joys of life as they crop up and blessed with abundance of wealth and vehicles, the person born under the **Durudhura** <note>original: "धुरुधुरा" (Durudhurā)</note> <term>yoga</term> is bountiful and waited upon by faithful attendants. But those born under the **Kemadruma** <note>original: "केमद्रुम" (Kemadruma)</note> <term>yoga</term>, though they may be of a princely race, become obscure, miserable, given to base ways, penurious, drudging as menials and wickedly-inclined.

->Excluding the Moon, the yogas called Vesi, Vasi, and Ubhayachari are formed<-
->By benefics in the 2nd, 12th, or both houses from the Sun; if by malefic planets, they are known as "Papa" (evil).<-
->With benefics on both sides of the rising sign, it is "Shubhakartari"; with malefics, it is the "Papa" pair.<-
->With benefics in the 2nd from the Ascendant, it is a very auspicious yoga, provided they are not aspected by malefics. || 8 ||<-

# Phaladeepika Chapter VI

*Verse 8* If benefic planets <note>planets that have a positive or favorable influence, such as Jupiter or Venus</note> other than the Moon occupy the 2nd, 12th or both the houses from the Sun, the resulting yogas <note>special planetary combinations or "unions"</note> are respectively termed **Subhavesi** (Good Vesi), **Subhavasi** (Good Vasi), and **Subhobhayachari** (Good Both-Sides). But if the planets in question be malefic <note>planets with a harsh or challenging influence, like Mars or Saturn</note> the concerned yogas are called **Papavesi** (Sinful Vesi) etc. When the 12th and the 2nd houses from the Lagna <note>the Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> are occupied by benefics, the yoga is **Subhakartari** (Benefic Scissors). It is called **Papakartari** (Malefic Scissors) when the above two houses are occupied by malefics. If benefics occupy the 2nd house from the Lagna unaspected by malefics, the yoga is termed **Susubha** (Very Auspicious).

->The person born under these yogas will be fortunate, happy, a treasury of virtues, brave, a king, and righteous; famous, beloved by all, exceedingly lucky, a donor, and a favorite of the ruler; possessing a beautiful body, pleasant speech, a lover of the world's variety, eloquent, renowned, and wealthy. These results should be understood for the Suvesi, Suvasi, and Ubhayachari yogas respectively in the order of the quarters of the verse.<-

*Verse 9* The person born in a **Suvesi** yoga will be very fortunate, happy, a store of merit, brave, a king and virtuous. He who is born in a **Suvasi** yoga will become famous, will be liked by all, will be exceedingly prosperous, liberal and the favourite of the sovereign. The man who has his birth in a **Subhayachari** yoga will have beautiful limbs, will be affable in address, will delight in everything, will be eloquent in his speech, renowned and wealthy.

->One who earns through injustice, a slanderer, lacking in appetite or taste, fond of the low-born, a wicked person, deceitful, a critic of others, associated with the vile, and well-versed in the scriptures of bad conduct; he will have a bad reputation in the world, a sorrowful mind, and be deprived of learning, wealth, and fortune. These are the results for those born in the Asubhavesi, Asubhavasi, and Asubhayachari yogas respectively.<-

*Verse 10* The person born in an **Asubhavesi** (Inauspicious Vesi)...

<insert>In the case of a person born under an Inauspicious Vesi</insert> yoga, they will unjustly vilify others; lose their splendor <note>or physical beauty</note>, will associate with the low and will be wicked. One who is born in an <term>Asubhavasi</term> <note>Inauspicious Vasi yoga, formed by malefic planets in the 12th house from the Sun</note> yoga will be a conjurer, will abuse others, and will befriend the wicked. His rules and precepts will incline towards wicked and unrighteous conduct. The man with an <term>Asubhobhayachari</term> <note>Inauspicious Ubhayachari yoga, where malefic planets flank both sides of the Sun</note> yoga will be mentally much afflicted owing to public dishonour. He will be bereft of learning, wealth and luck.

->The one born in an Auspicious Kartari yoga will be long-lived,<-
->free from fear, disease, and enemies, and shall be happy,<-
->prosperous, and endowed with wealth.<-
->One born in an Inauspicious Kartari yoga will be destitute, impure,<-

->and will meet an early death. || 11 ||<-
<note>original: "जैवातृको विभयरोगरिपु: सुखी स्या- दाढ्य: श्रिया च शुभकर्तरियोगजातः । नि:स्वोऽशुचिर्विसुखदारसुतोऽङ्गहीनः स्यात्पापकर्तरिभवोऽचिरमायुरेति ॥ ११ ॥"</note>

*Verse 11.* The person who has his birth in a <term>Subhakartari</term> <note>Auspicious "Scissor" yoga, where the rising sign or a planet is flanked by beneficial planets</note> yoga will be long-lived, fearless, free from disease, without enemies, happy and rich. He who is born in a <term>Papakartari</term> <note>Inauspicious "Scissor" yoga, where the subject is flanked by malefic planets</note> yoga will be poor, impure, unhappy, bereft of wife and children, deprived of some limb and short-lived.

->One who is well-behaved, of a religious mind, cheerful,<-
->fortunate, honored by kings,<-
->of a gentle nature, speaking with a smile,<-
->and wealthy, is born in the Amala yoga. || 12 ||<-
<note>original: "आचारवान् धर्ममति: प्रसन्न: सौभाग्यवान् पार्थिवमाननीयः । मृदुस्वभाव: स्मितभाषणश्च धनी भवेचामलयोगजातः ॥ १२ ॥"</note>

*Verse 12.* The person born in the <term>Amala</term> <note>Stainless or Pure yoga, formed when a benefic planet occupies the 10th house from the Moon or Ascendant</note> yoga will be virtuous, pious, kindly disposed, fortunate, highly revered by his sovereign, gentle, affable in his speech and wealthy.

->In the case of the Very Auspicious, Auspicious Kartari,<-
->Auspicious Vesi and others, as with the Sunapha yoga,<-
->the results should be understood through the benefics,<-
->and the opposite results through the malefic planets. || 13 ||<-
<note>original: "सुशुभे शुभकर्तर्यां वेश्यादौ सुनभादिवत् । शुभै: क्रमात्फलं ज्ञेयं विपरीतंमसद्ग्रहै: ॥ १३ ॥"</note>

*Verse 13.* In the case of <term>Susubha</term> <note>Very Auspicious</note>, <term>Subhakartari</term> <note>Auspicious Scissor</note>, <term>Subhavesi</term> <note>Auspicious Solar</note> and other yogas the effects given for <term>Sunapha</term> <note>A yoga formed by planets in the 2nd house from the Moon</note> and other yogas caused by beneficial planets should be applied; the opposite is true when caused by malefic planets.

<summary>This page details the moral and physical consequences of specific planetary alignments (yogas), contrasting the prosperity and health brought by "Auspicious" (Subha) combinations with the poverty and misfortune caused by "Inauspicious" (Papa) ones.</summary>

->If a male is born during the day and the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant are in odd signs, the Great Fortune yoga is formed.<-
->For women, this occurs in the opposite case (night birth, even signs). If Jupiter is in a quadrant from the Moon, it is Kesari.<-
->If the Moon is in the 12th, 8th, or 6th from Jupiter, it is Sakata, unless the Moon is in a quadrant from the Ascendant.<-
->The Adhama, Sama, and Varishta yogas are well-known when the Moon is in a quadrant, etc., from the Sun.<-

*Sloka 14.* If, at a day birth in the case of a male, the Sun, Moon and <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> are in odd signs <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc.</note>, the <term>Mahabhagya</term> <note>original: "महाभाग्य" - Great Fortune</note> yoga is formed. The Moon in a <term>Kendra</term> <note>A quadrant or angular house: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note> position to Jupiter brings on <term>Kesari</term> <note>original: "केसरी" - Lion yoga, signifying strength and endurance</note> yoga. The Moon in the 12th, 8th or 6th house from Jupiter causes <term>Sakata</term> <note>original: "शकट" - Cart yoga, implying a life of ups and downs like a rotating wheel</note>. But if the Moon be in a <term>Kendra</term> from the <term>Lagna</term>, there is no <term>Sakata</term>. <margin>||</margin> The <term>Adhama</term> <note>Lowly</note>, <term>Sama</term> <note>Moderate</note> and <term>Varishta</term> <note>Excellent</note> yogas are formed when the Moon occupies respectively a <term>Kendra</term>, a <term>Panaphara</term> <note>Succeedent houses: 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th</note> and an <term>Apoklima</term> <note>Cadent houses: 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th</note> house counted from the Sun.

->Like a lion destroying a pack of enemies, with mature speech in an assembly and a passionate nature;<-

*Verse 16.* The person born in the **Lion** <note>original: "Kesari"</note> yoga will destroy, like a lion, all his enemies. He will speak boldly in an assembly. He will be passionate and emotional in his behavior. He will be long-lived, highly renowned and exceedingly intelligent. He will conquer everything by his own valor.

->Occasionally falling from fortune, he again and again recovers all his luck;<-

*Verse 17.* The person born in the **Cart** <note>original: "Sakata"</note> yoga will often become unfortunate (lose his luck), and may again regain what he once lost. He will be a very ordinary and insignificant man in the world. He will attain much mental grief that is inevitable and will be exceedingly unhappy.

->In the Bad, Middling, and Best yogas, one finds wealth, vehicles, fame, and happiness;<-
->Knowledge, wisdom, modesty, skill, learning, generosity, and enjoyment yield fruits in the same manner. ॥ 18 ॥<-

*Verse 18.* According as the yoga at birth is **Bad** <note>original: "Kashta" or "Adhama" (lowest)</note>, **Middling** <note>original: "Madhyama" or "Sama" (equal/average)</note>, or **Excellent** <note>original: "Vara" or "Varishta" (best)</note>, the wealth, vehicles, fame, happiness, knowledge, intellectual precision, modesty, ability, learning, liberality and enjoyment of the person born will be at the lowest, middling or highest respectively.

->One becomes wealthy if all benefics are in the houses of increase from the Moon or the Ascendant;<-
->The Spotless (Amala) yoga is formed by benefics from the Moon or the Ascendant. <note>original: "Amala" means stainless or pure; "Upachaya" refers to the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses of growth.</note><-

# Phaladeepika Chapter VI

<page-num>54</page-num>
-> Light on the Fruits of Astrology, Chapter VI <-

If the lord of the Moon-sign is joined with the lord of the Ascendant in a quadrant and occupies the sign of a very friendly planet, and if a strong planet aspects the Ascendant, this is the <term>Pushkala</term> <note>meaning "abundant" or "magnificent"</note> yoga. || 19 ||

*Verse 19.* If all the benefic planets <note>Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and a strong Moon</note> occupy <term>Upachaya</term> <note>houses of improvement: the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th</note> houses, whether reckoned from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or rising sign</note> or the Moon, the resulting yoga is termed <term>Vasumat</term> <note>meaning "wealthy"</note>. When benefics occupy the 10th house counted from the <term>Lagna</term> or the Moon, the yoga is called <term>Amala</term> <note>meaning "pure" or "stainless"</note>. If the lord of the <term>Rasi</term> <note>Zodiac sign</note> occupied by the Moon in conjunction with the lord of the <term>Lagna</term> be posited in a <term>Kendra</term> <note>a quadrant or angular house: 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note> or in the house of a very friendly planet and aspect the <term>Lagna</term>, and if at the same time a planet possessing strength occupy the <term>Lagna</term>, the resulting yoga is called <term>Pushkala</term>.

One born in the Vasumat yoga will always stay in his own home and possess great wealth. In the Amala yoga, one becomes a ruler of the earth, wealthy, blessed with children, fame, and prosperity, and is highly ethical. One born in the Pushkala yoga is magnificent, honored by great kings, famous, adorned with fine clothes and ornaments, sweet-spoken, and a supreme lord. || 20 ||

*Verse 20.* The person born in the <term>Vasumat</term> yoga will always keep to his house <note>implying stability and landed property</note>, and will command plenty of money. He who has his birth in the <term>Amala</term> yoga will rule over the earth, will be wealthy, will have sons, will be prosperous and prudent. He who is born in the <term>Pushkala</term> yoga will be wealthy, will be honored by kings, and become famous. He will be decorated with beautiful ornaments and clothes. He will be sweet-tongued, supremely good, and a lord.

If all benefics are situated in five, six, or seven houses in a continuous row, it is called a <term>Mala</term> <note>Garland</note> yoga; similarly, if malefic planets are so placed in the 8th, 6th, and 12th houses, they are known by inauspicious names. If the lord of the 9th house and Venus are both in their own signs or in a quadrant or trine, it is <term>Lakshmi</term> yoga; if the Moon is so placed and aspected by Jupiter, it is <term>Gauri</term> yoga. || 21 ||

<summary>This page defines the planetary conditions and life outcomes for the Vasumat, Amala, and Pushkala yogas, emphasizing wealth, status, and character. It also introduces the Lakshmi and Gauri yogas, which are associated with prosperity and grace.</summary>

<keywords>Vasumat yoga, Amala yoga, Pushkala yoga, Upachaya houses, wealth, kingship, Phaladeepika, Lakshmi yoga, Gauri yoga, planetary combinations</keywords>

<page-num>55</page-num>
->### Verses 21-24 Chapter Six 55<-

*Verse 21.* If all the planets in regular order occupy the 5th, 6th and 7th houses, the <term>Yoga</term> <note>A specific planetary combination or "union" in a birth chart</note> is called **Auspicious Garland** <note>original: "Subhamala"</note>. But if they should be positioned in the 8th, 6th and 12th houses, it is called **Inauspicious Garland** <note>original: "Asubhamala"</note>. If the lord of the 9th house and Venus be positioned in their own or exaltation houses identical with a <term>Trikona</term> <note>A triangular house: the 1st, 5th, or 9th</note> or a <term>Kendra</term> <note>A cardinal house: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note>, the resulting yoga is **Lakshmi** <note>Named after the Goddess of wealth and prosperity</note>. If the Moon in the above position be aspected by Jupiter, the yoga is **Gouri** <note>Named after the Goddess of purity and motherhood</note>.

->The person born in the **Auspicious Garland** yoga will be a leader of people, praised by rulers, an enjoyer of life, and a generous giver. He assists others in their tasks, loves his relatives, is blessed with a virtuous wife and sons, and is courageous. (22)<-

*Verse 22.* The person born in a **Good Garland** <note>original: "Sumala"</note> yoga will be a governor (or director), will be extolled by kings and will be devoted to enjoyment. He will be liberal in gifts, helpful to others in getting their work done, and will love his relations. He will be blessed with a good wife and sons and will be courageous.

->One born in the **Inauspicious Garland** yoga follows evil paths, is miserable, harms others, and is ungrateful. He is cowardly, lacks devotion to the learned <note>original: "Brahmins"</note>, is condemned by the world, and loves strife. (23)<-

*Verse 23.* He who is born in an **Inauspicious Garland** <note>original: "Asubhamalika"</note> yoga will resort to bad ways, will be unhappy, will tease others, will be ungrateful, will be timid, will have no reverence for <term>Brahmins</term> <note>The priestly or scholarly class in traditional Indian society</note>, will be cursed by the public and will be fond of (promoting) quarrels.

->The one born under the **Lakshmi** yoga constantly delights with a virtuous woman, is free from disease, wealthy, and radiant. He protects his kinsmen and is a dwelling place for the grace of the Great Goddess Lakshmi. He travels by the best palanquins, horses, and elephants; he brings joy to the world, is a chosen ruler, and a donor. (24)<-

*Verse 24.* The person born in a **Lakshmi** yoga will ever be sporting with a damsel of a very amiable <insert>disposition</insert> <note>The text cuts off here, but the Sanskrit verse implies a woman of auspicious character</note>.

It contrasts the prosperous, virtuous life of those born under auspicious "garlands" with the miserable and contentious life of those born under inauspicious ones.</summary>

->If the lord of the Ascendant, the Sun, and the Moon are in a quadrant or a trine,<-

If Jupiter, the lord of the 5th and Saturn occupy similar positions, the yoga formed is called <term>Virinchi</term> <note>original: "विरिञ्चि"; an epithet of the God Brahma, the Creator</note>.

# Chapter Six

his speech and will possess much wealth, wife and sons. He will shine with priestly <note>original: "Brahminical"</note> lustre. He will live long and have his senses under control and will be saluted by kings.

->When two planets, each being the lord of a house, occupy each other's signs,<-
->starting from the Ascendant to the twelfth house, sixty-six such combinations are described by the wise.<-
->Thirty of these are called "Miserable" combinations, arising from the lords of the twelfth, sixth, and eighth houses;<-
->eight are called "Wicked" combinations arising from the lord of the third house; the remaining are known as "Great" combinations. (Verse 32)<-

*Verse 32.* When two planets each owning a <term>bhava</term> <note>a "house" or astrological sector representing specific life areas</note> mutually interchange places, *i. e.*, each occupy the other's house, the action is termed <term>Parivartana</term> <note>an "exchange of signs"</note>; and there are 66 such interchanges caused by the several pairs of house-lords beginning from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or 1st house</note> and ending with the 12th. Out of these, 30 are caused by the lords of the 6th, 8th and 12th <note>traditionally considered the "dusthanas" or difficult houses</note> and are termed <term>Dainya</term> <note>Miserable or Poverty-stricken</note> yogas; and 8 are caused by the lord of the 3rd, thus:—

The lord of the 12th occupying any one of the other 11 houses and the lord of this latter house occupying the 12th ... 11

The lord of the 6th occupying any one of the remaining 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10 and 11 houses and the lord of this occupying the 6th ... 10

The lord of the 8th occupying any one of the remaining 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10 and 11 houses and the lord of this occupying the 8th ... 9

The lord of the 3rd occupying any one of the remaining 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10 and 11 houses and the lord of this occupying the 3rd ... 8

---

These 8 are called <term>Khala</term> <note>Wicked or Mischievous</note> yogas. The remaining 28 are termed <term>Maha</term> <note>Great or Auspicious</note> yogas.

<summary>This page defines the 66 types of Parivartana Yogas (planetary exchanges), categorizing them into Dainya (miserable), Khala (wicked), and Maha (great) combinations based on the houses involved.</summary>
<keywords>Parivartana Yoga, house exchange, Dainya Yoga, Khala Yoga, Maha Yoga, astrological houses, planetary lords, Sanskrit astrology</keywords>

They are formed thus —

| | |
| :--- | :--- |
| The lord of the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth.</note> occupying any one of the remaining 2, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10 and 11 houses and the lord of this house occupying the Lagna ... ... ... | 7 |
| The lord of the 2nd house occupying any one of the remaining 6 houses, viz., 4, 5, 7, 9, 10 and 11 and the lord of this latter occupying the 2nd ... ... .. | 6 |
| The lord of the 4th house occupying any one of 5, 7, 9, 10 and 11 houses and the lord of this occupying the 4th ... ... | 5 |
| The lord of the 5th house occupying any one of 7, 9, 10 and 11 houses and the lord of this occupying the 5th ... ... | 4 |
| The lord of the 7th occupying any one of 9, 10 and 11 and the lord of this occupying the 7th ... ... | 3 |
| The lord of the 9th occupying the 10th or 11th house and the lord of this occupying the 9th .. .. | 2 |
| The lord of the 10th occupying the 11th and *vice versa* .. | 1 |
| | --- |
| | 28 |

-><note>original: "मूर्ख: स्यादपवादको दुरितकृन्नित्यं सपत्नार्दित:..."</note>
The person may be a fool, a slanderer, a doer of evil, and constantly harassed by enemies;
Speaking harshly, born to wretchedness, fickle-minded, and interrupted in his efforts.
One born in a Khala yoga is sometimes arrogant, yet may occasionally obtain full fortune;
Sometimes speaking mildly, yet at other times suffering the inauspiciousness of poverty and grief. || 33 ||<-

*Verse 33.* The man born in a <term>Dainya</term><note>Literally "miserable" or "wretched"; a yoga formed by an exchange between the lord of a difficult house and a favorable one.</note> yoga will be a fool, will be reviling others and commit sinful actions. He will always be tormented by his enemies, will speak woundingly and will be unsteady in mind. Interruptions will arise on all his undertakings. The man born in the <term>Khala</term><note>Literally "wicked" or "mischievous"; a yoga suggesting a fluctuating or unstable character.</note> yoga will at one time become haughty and foul tongued, while at another time gentle in speech. Sometimes he will attain all kinds of prosperity, while at other times he will have to endure much poverty, misery and the like.

<summary>This page details the 28 varieties of Maha Yogas through planetary exchanges and describes the negative or fluctuating life outcomes associated with the Dainya and Khala Yogas.</summary>

<page-num>51</page-num>
<header>Verses 34-37 Chapter Six 51</header>

The person born under the influence of the <term>Maha</term> <note>original: "महा" (Great)</note> yoga will be the dwelling place of the graceful glances of the Goddess <term>Sri</term> <note>original: "श्री"; the Goddess of wealth and fortune</note>. He will be a powerful lord and wealthy, dressing in multicolored garments and gold ornaments. He will receive great honors from his sovereign and possess vehicles, wealth, children, and cattle. ॥ 34 ॥

*Verse 34.* The person born in a <term>Maha</term> yoga will be the repository of the blessings of the Goddess <term>Sri</term> and will be a lord and wealthy. He will wear cloths of variegated colour and bedeck himself with gold ornaments. He will receive rich presents from his sovereign and certain powers (authority) also will be conferred on him. He will command vehicles, wealth and children.

If the lord of the sign occupied by the lord of the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> is placed in its own sign or its sign of exaltation, and is located in a <term>Kona</term> <note>A trine house; the 1st, 5th, or 9th</note> or <term>Kendra</term> <note>An angular house; the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note>, that yoga is known as <term>Kahala</term>. Similarly, if the lord of the sign occupied by the lord of the <term>Lagna</term> is situated in a similar manner, the yoga is called <term>Parvata</term>.

*Verse 35* Find where the lord of the <term>Rasi</term> <note>Zodiac sign</note> occupied by the lord of the <term>Lagna</term> is posited. If the lord of this <term>Rasi</term> be in his exaltation or own sign identical with a <term>Kona</term> or <term>Kendra</term>, the yoga is called <term>Kahala</term> <note>original: "काहल"</note>. If the lord of the sign occupied by the lord of the <term>Lagna</term> be similarly situated, the resulting yoga is termed <term>Parvata</term> <note>original: "पर्वत"; meaning Mountain</note>.

One born in the <term>Kahala</term> yoga is prosperous, wise, benevolent, and brings safety; he is honored by kings. One born in the <term>Parvata</term> yoga possesses enduring wealth and happiness, performs lasting deeds, and becomes a ruler of the earth. ॥ 36 ॥

*Verse 36* He who is born in the <term>Kahala</term> yoga will thrive well, will be noble, benevolent, kind and propitious. He will be respected by other men. The person who is born in the <term>Parvata</term> yoga will have everlasting wealth and happiness. He will do acts causing eternal benefit. He will become the lord of the Earth.

If the lords of the 9th and 10th houses are joined together and occupy auspicious divisions, it is called <term>Raja Yoga</term> <note>A "Royal Union" indicating high status or political power</note>. Similarly, a union between the lords of a <term>Kendra</term> and a <term>Kona</term> results in <term>Shankha</term> yoga. ॥ 37 ॥

<summary>This page defines and describes the fruits of several auspicious astrological combinations: Maha, Kahala, Parvata, Raja, and Shankha Yogas. These configurations generally promise wealth, royal favor, and a virtuous character to the individual.</summary>
<keywords>Maha yoga, Sri, Kahala yoga, Parvata yoga, Lagna, Rasi, Kona, Kendra, Shankha yoga, Raja yoga, Vedic astrology, planetary combinations</keywords>

# Phaladeepika Chapter VI

*Verse 37.* If the lords of the 9th <note>the house of fortune and piety</note> and 10th houses <note>the house of profession and honor</note> occupy in conjunction an auspicious <term>Bhava</term> <note>a house or astrological division</note>, it constitutes <term>Raja Yoga</term> <note>a combination for royal status or great success</note>. If the lords of a <term>Kendra</term> <note>quadrant houses: 1, 4, 7, or 10</note> and a <term>Kona</term> <note>trine houses: 1, 5, or 9</note> be similarly placed, i.e., be in conjunction in an auspicious house, the resulting yoga is called <term>Sankha</term> <note>literally "Conch," symbolizing fame and prosperity</note>.

->Accompanied by the sounds of kettledrums and conches, with a circular umbrella made of soft cloth held aloft,<-
->With elephants, horses, and palanquins, the king is praised by bards;<-
->With hands filled with various gifts offered by noble men,<-
->The king born in Sankha yoga will be full of wealth and the enjoyment of many excellent women.<-

*Verse 38.* The person who has his birth in a <term>Raja</term> yoga will be a king who (when he sets out) will be accompanied by elephants, horses, litters, palanquins and the like, the roaring of kettle drums and the sounds of the conch shell, with a circular umbrella made of soft woven cloth held over him, praised by bards and minstrels <note>original: "Magadha," referring to a specific class of court bards</note> and solicited by eminent men with various kinds of presents in their hands. The person born in a <term>Sankha</term> yoga will enjoy all comforts in the company of many beautiful damsels.

->The Sankhya yogas are seven: Vallaki, Dama, Pasa,<-
->Kedara, Sula, Yuga, and Gola, formed by planets in seven down to one sign,<-
->Excluding those other yogas previously mentioned.<-

*Verse 39.* <term>Vallaki</term> <note>Lute</note> or <term>Veena</term>, <term>Dama</term> <note>Wreath</note>, <term>Pasa</term> <note>Noose</note>, <term>Kedara</term> <note>Field</note>, <term>Sula</term> <note>Spear</note>, <term>Yuga</term> <note>Yoke</note> and <term>Gola</term> <note>Globe</note> are the seven <term>Sankhya</term> <note>numerical</note> yogas respectively produced by the seven planets occupying as many <term>Rasis</term> <note>Zodiac signs</note> as are denoted by the seven figures commencing from seven and diminishing successively by one, i.e., by the numbers 7, 6, 5, 4, 3, 2 and 1. These <term>Sankhya</term> yogas are to be reckoned only when those mentioned previously <note>such as the Nabhasa yogas</note> are absent.

<summary>This page defines the prestigious Raja and Sankha yogas formed by the lords of specific houses and introduces the seven Sankhya (numerical) yogas based on how many signs the seven planets occupy.</summary>

->**Verses 40-43 Chapter Six**<-

In the <term>Veena</term> <note>original: वीणा; a stringed musical instrument</note> yoga, one is fond of dancing and music and seeks wealth. In the <term>Dama</term> <note>original: दाम; meaning a cord or garland</note> yoga, one is a generous giver, a ruler, and a benefactor. In the <term>Pasa</term> <note>original: पाश; meaning a noose</note> yoga, one is a seeker of pleasure, possesses a good character, and supports kinsmen. In the <term>Kedara</term> <note>original: केदार; meaning a field</note> yoga, one is associated with the fields of Lord Krishna <note>interpreted here as being endowed with agricultural wealth</note>. ||40||

*Verse 40.* The person born in the <term>Veena</term> <term>yoga</term> <note>a specific planetary alignment</note> will be fond of dancing and music and will be wealthy. The man born in the <term>Dama</term> <term>yoga</term> will be very liberal, a king and a benefactor. The person whose birth takes place in the <term>Pasa</term> <term>yoga</term> will be opulent, devoted to enjoyment, and kindly disposed towards his relations. The man who has the <term>Kedara</term> <term>yoga</term> in his nativity will be endowed with wealth and pursue agriculture.

In the <term>Sula</term> <note>original: शूल; meaning a spear or trident</note> yoga, one is harmful, of an angry temperament, and poor. One is a heretic and devoid of wealth in the <term>Yuga</term> <note>original: युग; meaning a pair or yoke</note> yoga. One born in the <term>Gola</term> <note>original: गोल; meaning a sphere or globe</note> yoga is destitute, sinful, associates with outcasts, is a poor craftsman, lazy, and short-lived. ||41||

*Verse 41.* The person born in the <term>Sula</term> <term>yoga</term> will be mischievous, of an angry temperament, and indigent. The man whose birth is in the <term>Yuga</term> <term>yoga</term> will be heretical and without wealth. He who is born in the <term>Gola</term> <term>yoga</term> will be without wealth, will commit sinful deeds, and associate with low people. He will be a bad artisan, indolent and short-lived.

When benefic planets are situated in the 7th, 6th, and 8th houses from the Moon, or similarly from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or rising sign</note>, it is <term>Adhiyoga</term>. One becomes a leader, a minister, or a king respectively; famous, prosperous, long-lived, and high-minded. ||42||

*Verse 42.* When benefic planets occupy the 7th, the 6th and the 8th places reckoned from the Moon or the <term>Lagna</term>, there results what is called the <term>Adhiyoga</term> <note>original: अधियोग; a "superior" or "additional" combination</note> wherein takes place the birth of a Commander (Police Superintendent or head), a minister or a ruler (of a District or Province) respectively. He who is born in the <term>Adhiyoga</term> becomes famous, prosperous, wealthy, long-lived and high-souled.

One born in <term>Adhiyoga</term> becomes a lord of men, with stable wealth and the sustainer of many relations. By this yoga, enemies are conquered, and one attains a long life and great fame. ||43||

*Verse 43* The person born in the **Adhiyoga** <note>original: "अधियोग" (Adhiyoga); a configuration where benefics are placed in the 6th, 7th, and 8th houses from the Moon or Ascendant</note> will become the lord of men, will be wealthy throughout life (have everlasting wealth), will feed many of his relations, will overthrow his enemies, enjoy long life and become widely renowned.

### When the houses are occupied or aspected by benefics, and their lords are placed in good positions, bright,
### and in their exaltation or own signs, twelve Yogas are formed for the houses starting from the first.
### Their names are Chamara, Dhenu, Saurya, Jaladhi, Chhatra, Astra, Kama, Asura,
### Bhagya, Khyati, Suparijata, and Musala, as declared by those learned in this science. || 44 ||

<margin>Verse 44</margin>
*Verse 44* **Houses** <note>original: "Bhavas"; the twelve divisions of the zodiac representing different areas of life</note> being occupied or aspected by benefics, their lords occupying good houses <note>usually the 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 5th, or 9th houses</note> or being in their exaltation or **own sign** <note>original: "swakshetra"</note> constitute certain **planetary combinations** <note>original: "yogas"</note> and there are 12 such for the 12 **houses** from the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagna"</note> onwards. Their names are in their order: (1) **Chamara** (The Fly-whisk), (2) **Dhenu** (The Cow), (3) **Saurya** (Heroism), (4) **Jaladhi** (The Ocean), (5) **Chhatra** (The Parasol), (6) **Astra** (The Weapon), (7) **Kama** (Desire), (8) **Asura** (The Spirit/Demon), (9) **Bhagya** (Fortune), (10) **Khyati** (Fame), (11) **Suparijata** (The Celestial Tree), and (12) **Musala** (The Pestle).

### Growing in greatness every day like the waxing moon, of a beautiful character,
### Famous, a leader of men, long-lived, and a treasury of wealth is one born in Chamara. || 45 ||

*Verse 45* The person born in the **Chamara yoga** <note>original: "चामरयोग"</note> will every day be growing in importance like the waxing Moon and will be of a virtuous disposition. He will become famous, a leader of men, long-lived and a receptacle for the Goddess **Sri** <note>original: "श्री"; the Goddess of wealth and prosperity</note>.

### Possessing wealth in food and drink, proficient in all sciences, with a great abundance of family,
### Rich in gold, gems, wealth, and grain, he shines like a king of kings in the Dhenu yoga. || 46 ||

<margin>---</margin>
*Verse 46* The person born in the **Dhenu** (The Cow) yoga will command good food, drink, etc. He will be wealthy, <insert>highly educated in all branches of knowledge, and possess a large, prosperous family.</insert>

# Phaladeepika, Chapter VI

...will be happy, wealthy, renowned, of good speech and learned. He will become a minister to a king. He will be keen-witted and respected by others.

->He will subdue very powerful enemies by force and through cruel actions; he will be honored but arrogant. His body will be marked by scars, yet he will be quarrelsome and possess a very strong physique if born under the **Weapon** <note>original: "Astra"</note> Yoga.<-

**Verse 50** The person who has his birth in the <term>Astra</term> (Weapon) yoga will forcibly subdue his very powerful foes. He will be rough in his behavior and arrogant. He will have a bruised limb, but will possess a strong body. He will be quarrelsome.

->He will be averse to the wives of others and will be supported by an excellent wife, children, and kinsmen. Surpassing his father in virtuous qualities, the one born of **Desire** <note>original: "Kama"</note> attains great and venerable wealth.<-

**Verse 51** The person born in the <term>Kama</term> (Desire) yoga will not even cast a look at other people's wives. He will be blessed with an excellent wife, children and relations. With his good qualities, he will shine better than his father, and he will rise to a very exalted position.

->He destroys the work of others, is a backbiter, and is solely intent on his own interests; he will be poor and stubborn. Suffering from a series of misfortunes of his own making, the one born in the **Demon** <note>original: "Asura"</note> yoga performs wicked deeds.<-

**Verse 52** The man born in the <term>Asura</term> (Demon) yoga will spoil other's work and will become a tale-bearer. He will be intent on securing his own interests. He will be poor but head-strong. He will do mean acts and become unhappy as a result of his own mischievous doings.

->Accompanied by the sound of waving fans and musical instruments, and possessing a permanent palanquin and wealth, he is saluted by great men and remains on the path of righteousness. He pleases the ancestors, the gods, and the community of the twice-born <note>The "twice-born" or Dvija refers to the upper social classes in classical India who have undergone spiritual initiation.</note> with devoted and excellent worship; he is of good conduct, an uplifter of his family, and kind-hearted if born under the **Fortune** <note>original: "Bhagya"</note> yoga.<-

**Verse 53** <note>This verse describes the Bhagya Yoga, associated with the 9th house of the zodiac, representing luck and piety.</note>

*Verse 53* He who has his birth in the <term>Bhagya (Fortune)</term> <term>yoga (planetary combination)</term> will move in a palanquin <note>A covered litter carried by poles on the shoulders of bearers, a sign of high status</note> in the midst of sounds caused by musical instruments and with <term>chowries</term> <note>Fly-whisks made of yak-tail hair, used as symbols of royal or divine authority</note> waving to and fro. ->Always purified by auspicious rites, a lord of the earth, with a hoard of accumulated wealth and a good family; devoted to hearing sacred stories and wise, the one born in <term>Parijata</term> is a source of happiness.<-

*Verse 55.* The person born in the <term>Parijata (Celestial Tree)</term> yoga will always be in the midst of festivities (or auspicious ceremonies), will become a king, will become the lord of much hoarded wealth, will own a large family, will be intent on hearing stories of great people, will be learned and doing something auspicious.

->Possessing wealth acquired through great hardship, humiliated, with fickle fortune yet inclined to proper expenditure; he attains heaven in his final state, though the one born in <term>Musala</term> is a rogue and restless.<-

*Verse 56.* The man born in the <term>Musala (Pestle)</term> yoga will become the owner of wealth earned with much difficulty; he will suffer humiliation; his wealth will be <note>The text breaks off here, likely continuing on the next page to describe the instability of his riches.</note>

<margin>12 Yogas</margin>
If the lords of the various houses <note>original: "bhavas"</note> starting from the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note> occupy the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, or if these houses are joined with or aspected by malefic planets, twelve types of yogas are said to arise for the twelve houses respectively. They are, in order: (1) <term>Avayoga</term> (Yoga of Misfortune), (2) <term>Nisswayoga</term> (Yoga of Poverty), (3) <term>Mrityu Yoga</term> (Yoga of Death/Destruction), (4) <term>Kuhu Yoga</term> (Yoga of the New Moon/Hidden), (5) <term>Pamara Yoga</term> (Yoga of the Lowly), (6) <term>Harsha Yoga</term> (Yoga of Joy), (7) <term>Dushkriti Yoga</term> (Yoga of Evil Action), (8) <term>Sarala Yoga</term> (Yoga of Simplicity/Straightforwardness), (9) <term>Nirbhagya Yoga</term> (Yoga of Ill-fortune), (10) <term>Duryoga</term> (Yoga of Hardship), (11) <term>Daridra Yoga</term> (Yoga of Penury), and (12) <term>Vimala Yoga</term> (Yoga of Purity).

<margin>} 12</margin>
**Verse 57** <note>original: "Sloka 57"</note>

---

**Verse 58**
One born under <term>Avayoga</term> <note>Yoga of Misfortune; occurs when the lord of the 1st house is in the 6th, 8th, or 12th</note> will be insignificant and lack fame. He will suffer from extreme, unbearable poverty and will have a short life. He will be humiliated by wicked people, possess a bad character, have a deformed body, and his status in life will be unstable.

---

**Verse 59**
The person born under <term>Nisswayoga</term> <note>Yoga of Poverty; occurs when the lord of the 2nd house is in the 6th, 8th, or 12th</note> will lack kind or pleasant speech. His family life will be fruitless or barren, and he will keep company with bad people. He will have poor eyesight and be devoid of intelligence, children, learning, and wealth. His riches will be snatched away by his enemies.

<summary>This page introduces twelve specific astrological combinations (yogas) that occur when house lords are placed in "dusthanas" (unfavorable houses), specifically defining Avayoga and Nisswayoga. These yogas generally describe various forms of hardship, poverty, and lack of social standing based on which house lord is afflicted.</summary>

associates, will have bad teeth and eyes, he will be wanting in intelligence <note>original: "intelligen e"</note>, will have few issues <note>original: "i sues"; meaning children</note>, will have no learning, and no power. He will be robbed of his wealth by his enemies.

->Vanquished by enemies, deprived of brothers, shameless in mind, with destroyed strength and wealth; weary from the fatigue of improper deeds, and of a deformed or excited character—such is one born in Mriti Yoga.<-

*Verse <note>original: "Slol a"</note> 60* The person who has his birth in a **Death** <note>Sanskrit: मृति (Mriti) yoga; a planetary combination associated with the 8th house of the zodiac, often relating to loss or transformation</note> yoga will be vanquished by his enemies, will have no brothers, will be devoid of shame, strength and wealth, wearied by fatigue caused by doing improper acts and will be of an excited temperament.

->Deprived of mother, vehicles, friends, happiness, ornaments, and kinsmen; without a stable position, or losing the place he occupies; devoted to a low woman—this is one born in Kuhu Yoga.<-

*Verse 61* The man born in a **New Moon** <note>Sanskrit: कुहूयोग (Kuhu yoga); associated with the 4th house, governing the home and emotions</note> yoga will be bereft of mother, vehicles, friends, happiness, ornaments and relations, will be without a situation having lost the one already got and will be attached to a low female.

->Living in misery, speaking untruths, lacking discrimination, a deceiver, with dead children or childless; an atheist who resorts to mean people and is a glutton—this is one born in Pamara Yoga.<-

*Verse 62* He who is born in a **Wicked** <note>Sanskrit: पामरयोग (Pamara) yoga; associated with the 5th house, relating to intelligence and progeny</note> yoga will have a miserable living and will be undiscriminating. He will be a liar and a cheat. He will lose children or will have no issue at all. He will resort to low people. He will be an atheist and a glutton.

->Endowed with happiness, enjoyment, and fortune; with a strong body, a conqueror of enemies, and fearful of sin; beloved of famous and chief men, possessing wealth, luster, fame, and sons—this is one born in Harsha Yoga.<-

*Verse 63* The person born in a **Joyful** <note>Sanskrit: हर्षयोग (Harsha) yoga; a positive combination associated with the 6th house, indicating the overcoming of difficulties</note> yoga will be endowed with happiness, enjoyment, good

# ॥ Chapter Seven ॥

->When three or more planets are in their signs of exaltation or in their own signs, and are situated in the cardinal houses, the person becomes a famous king.<-
->If five or more such planets are present, even one born in a different lineage becomes a lord of the earth, possessed of a multitude of elephants. ॥ 1 ॥<-

## Chapter VII.

*Sloka 1* The person born with three or more planets in exaltation or <term>Swakshetra<note>A planet's "own sign" where it is comfortable and strong.</note></term> and at the same time posited in <term>Kendras</term> will become a king widely renowned. If there are five or more such planets in a nativity they will make the person, even if born in an ordinary family, a king (ruler of the world) endowed with numerous elephants and horses.

->Those born in royal families become kings if they are not born under bad combinations;<-
->if the planets are not eclipsed, even those not born in royal families certainly shine as kings.<-
->With three or more planets in the cardinal houses, in their own or exalted signs, those of royal birth become kings;<-
->while others born in different families become equal to a king, or sometimes kings themselves. ॥ 2 ॥<-

*Sloka 2* Persons born in a royal family will become kings if, at the time of birth, they are not born under any <term>Duryoga<note>original: "दुर्योग" — an inauspicious planetary combination that brings hardship or cancels good fortune.</note></term> or if the planets be not eclipsed by the Sun's rays. Three or more planets in <term>Kendra</term> positions at birth identical with their own or exaltation signs will

*Verse 3.* Even a single planet though in depression <note>the sign where a planet is at its weakest</note> is capable of making the person born a king's equal if he be with brilliant rays, retrograde in motion and occupy an auspicious house (*i. e.*, other than the 6th, 8th or 12th). *Verse 9.* If at the birth of a person Mars occupy <term>Mesha</term> (Aries), <term>Simha</term> (Leo) or <term>Dhanus</term> (Sagittarius) identical with the <term>Lagna</term>, and be aspected by a friendly planet, a ruler of the earth is ushered into the world.
